# List 18

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>patrician</b></td><td>/pəˈtrɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贵族，名门望族</td><td>an old patrician family<br>古老的贵族家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She came from an old patrician family that once owned half the county.  她出身于一个曾拥有半个县的古老贵族家庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aristocrat</td><td>/əˈrɪstəkræt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>贵族，贵族出身者</td><td>a landed aristocrat<br>拥有地产的贵族</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The estate once belonged to a ruined Russian aristocrat.  这处宅邸曾属于一位没落的俄国贵族。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noble</td><td>/ˈnoʊbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>高贵的，贵族的</td><td>a noble bearing<br>高贵的气派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horse moved with a noble grace across the field.  那匹马以高贵的优雅姿态穿过田野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blueblood</td><td>/ˈbluːblʌd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贵族，名门出身者</td><td>an old New England blueblood<br>新英格兰的老世家子弟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club admitted only bluebloods until well after the war.  这家俱乐部战后很久仍只收名门子弟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aristocratic</td><td>/əˌrɪstəˈkrætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贵族的，高贵的</td><td>aristocratic manners<br>贵族式的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His aristocratic manners made the other guests uneasy.  他贵族式的举止让其他客人不太自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 genteel</td><td>/dʒenˈtiːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，不粗俗的</td><td>genteel manners<br>有教养的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her genteel accent betrayed an expensive and very traditional upbringing.  她文雅的口音透露出昂贵而传统的成长背景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gentle</td><td>/ˈdʒentəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>温和的，轻柔的</td><td>a gentle voice<br>温柔的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave the door a gentle push and stepped inside.  他轻轻推了一下门，走了进去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grand</td><td>/ˈɡrænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，壮丽的</td><td>a grand staircase<br>宏伟的楼梯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held the ceremony in a grand marble hall.  他们在宏伟的大理石厅里举行仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 great</td><td>/ˈɡreɪt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>显赫的，地位高的</td><td>the great families<br>显赫的家族</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The great families of the city controlled every guild.  城中的显赫家族控制着每个行会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 highborn</td><td>/ˈhaɪˌbɔrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>出身高贵的</td><td>a highborn lady<br>出身高贵的女子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play mocks a highborn lady who cannot cook.  这部剧嘲讽一位不会做饭的贵族女子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 highbred</del></td><td>/ˈhaɪˌbrɛd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>出身良好的，教养好的</td><td>highbred courtesy<br>良好教养的礼貌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He treated the servants with highbred courtesy.  他以良好教养的礼貌对待仆人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 upper-class</td><td>/ˌʌpər ˈklæs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>上流社会的，上层阶级的</td><td>an upper-class accent<br>上流社会的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His upper-class accent marked him out in the factory.  他上流社会的口音让他在工厂里格外显眼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wellborn</td><td>/ˈwelbɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>出身高贵的</td><td>a wellborn officer<br>出身高贵的军官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only wellborn officers could once buy such commissions.  从前只有出身高贵的军官才能买到这种官职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plebeian</td><td>/pləˈbiːən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平民的，社会下层的</td><td>plebeian tastes in music<br>平民化的音乐趣味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic sneered at the plebeian tastes of the weekend audience.  评论家嘲笑周末观众平民化的趣味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>patriot</b></td><td>/ˈpeɪtriət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>爱国者</td><td>a devoted patriot<br>赤诚的爱国者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He considered himself a patriot who would defend his country at any cost.  他自认为是不惜一切代价保卫祖国的爱国者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loyalist</td><td>/ˈlɔɪəlɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>忠诚者，效忠者</td><td>a party loyalist<br>党内忠诚分子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a few loyalists stayed with the leader after the split.  分裂后只有少数忠诚者仍跟随领袖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nationalist</td><td>/ˈnæʃənələst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>民族主义者，国家主义者</td><td>a fervent nationalist<br>狂热的民族主义者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nationalist won the election by promising closed borders.  这位民族主义者以承诺封闭边境赢得选举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partisan</td><td>/ˈpɑːrtəzn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>党派偏见的；盲目拥护者</td><td>partisan politics<br>党派偏见的政治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate quickly sank into partisan shouting about very old grievances.  辩论很快沦为带党派偏见的叫嚷，纠缠陈年旧怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apostate</td><td>/əˈpɑːsteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>叛教者，背弃信念者</td><td>denounced as an apostate<br>被斥为叛教者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Former comrades denounced him as an apostate and a liar.  昔日同志斥他为叛徒和骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 recreant</del></td><td>/ˈrɛkriənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>背叛者，怯懦的叛徒</td><td>branded a recreant<br>被打上叛徒烙印</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ballad calls him a recreant who fled the battle.  这首民谣称他是逃离战场的怯懦叛徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 renegade</td><td>/ˈrenɪɡeɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>背教者，叛徒</td><td>a renegade from the church<br>背教出走者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Once a devoted monk, he became a renegade and denounced the church openly.  他曾是虔诚的修士，后来成了背教者并公开谴责教会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 traitor</td><td>/ˈtreɪtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>叛徒，卖国者</td><td>hanged as a traitor<br>作为叛徒被处死</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History records him as a traitor to his own city.  历史把他记为背叛自己城市的叛徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 turncoat</td><td>/ˈtɜːrnkoʊt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>叛徒</td><td>a political turncoat<br>政治上的变节者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old comrades denounced him as a turncoat after he joined the rival party.  他加入敌对政党后，老战友们谴责他是叛徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>patronize</b></td><td>/ˈpeɪtrənaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞助；以高人一等的态度对待</td><td>patronize the arts<br>赞助艺术</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wealthy merchants patronized young painters throughout the Renaissance.  富商在整个文艺复兴时期都赞助年轻画家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advocate</td><td>/ˈædvəkeɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持，提倡</td><td>advocate reform<br>提倡改革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many doctors advocate walking as the simplest form of exercise.  许多医生提倡把走路作为最简单的运动方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 back</td><td>/ˈbæk/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，资助</td><td>back a candidate<br>支持某位候选人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several investors agreed to back her new company.  几位投资人同意资助她的新公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 champion</td><td>/ˈtʃæmpiən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持</td><td>champion a cause<br>支持一项事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has championed the rights of migrant workers for years.  她多年来一直为农民工的权益奔走呼吁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endorse</td><td>/ɪnˈdɔːrs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开支持</td><td>endorse a candidate<br>公开支持某候选人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union voted to endorse the mayor for a second term.  工会投票支持市长连任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 support</td><td>/səˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持，支撑</td><td>support a proposal<br>支持一项提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most members voted to support the new proposal.  大多数成员投票支持这项新提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uphold</td><td>/ʌpˈhoʊld/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，赞成；维持（原判）</td><td>uphold a decision<br>维持原判决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The appeals court chose to uphold the original ruling in full.  上诉法院决定完全维持原判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 condescend</td><td>/ˌkɑːndɪˈsend/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>居高临下地对待；俯就</td><td>condescend to his students<br>对学生摆架子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecturer condescended to his students in every class.  这位讲师每节课都对学生摆架子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 baffle</td><td>/ˈbæfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使疑惑</td><td>baffle the experts<br>使专家困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange symptoms continued to baffle the visiting experts.  这些奇怪的症状一直使来访的专家疑惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foil</td><td>/fɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>挫败</td><td>foil a plot<br>挫败阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Alert guards foiled the plot before anyone was hurt.  警觉的警卫在有人受伤之前挫败了阴谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 frustrate</td><td>/ˈfrʌstreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，使受挫；使沮丧</td><td>frustrate one's plans<br>使计划受挫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow frustrated our plans to drive home.  大雪打乱了我们开车回家的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sabotage</td><td>/ˈsæbətɑːʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>妨害，破坏</td><td>an act of sabotage<br>一次破坏行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators concluded that the fire was an act of deliberate sabotage.  调查人员认定这场火灾是一次故意的破坏行动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>paucity</b></td><td>/ˈpɔːsəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，缺乏</td><td>a paucity of data<br>数据匮乏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paucity of reliable data made the researchers cautious about conclusions.  可靠数据的匮乏使研究者对结论十分谨慎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dearth</td><td>/dɜːrθ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>供应不足</td><td>a dearth of talent<br>人才匮乏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a real dearth of affordable housing in this district.  这个区确实缺乏买得起的住房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deficit</td><td>/ˈdefəsət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>不足，亏空</td><td>a severe water deficit<br>严重的水资源不足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The region faces a serious water deficit after three dry years.  该地区在三个干旱年后面临严重缺水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drought</td><td>/ˈdraʊt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>长期缺乏；旱灾</td><td>a drought of new ideas<br>新思想的匮乏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The studio suffered a long drought of good scripts.  这家制片厂长期缺乏好剧本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inadequacy</td><td>/ɪnˈædɪkwəsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不足，能力不够</td><td>a sense of inadequacy<br>能力不足感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report exposed the inadequacy of existing safety rules.  报告揭示了现有安全规定的不足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insufficiency</td><td>/ˌɪnsəˈfɪʃənsiː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不足，不充分</td><td>insufficiency of evidence<br>证据不足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The case was dismissed because of the insufficiency of the evidence.  由于证据不足，案件被驳回。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lack</td><td>/ˈlæk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>缺乏，不足</td><td>a lack of funds<br>资金不足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project stalled because of a serious lack of funds.  项目因严重缺乏资金而停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 scantiness</del></td><td>/ˈskæntinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不足，稀少</td><td>the scantiness of rainfall<br>降雨稀少</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scantiness of the harvest worried farmers across the whole valley.  收成稀少让整个山谷的农民忧心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scarcity</td><td>/ˈskersɪti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>稀缺，不足</td><td>a scarcity of housing<br>住房稀缺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A scarcity of clean water threatens millions of people every year.  清洁水源的稀缺每年威胁数百万人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shortage</td><td>/ˈʃɔːrtədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>短缺，不足</td><td>a shortage of nurses<br>护士短缺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A shortage of nurses forced the ward to close early.  护士短缺迫使病房提前关闭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 undersupply</del></td><td>/ˌʌndərsəˈplaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>供应不足</td><td>an undersupply of nurses<br>护士供应不足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A chronic undersupply of nurses has strained the country's hospitals.  护士长期供应不足使全国医院不堪重负。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 want</td><td>/ˈwɑːnt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>缺乏，短缺</td><td>for want of evidence<br>因缺乏证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project collapsed for want of steady funding and clear leadership.  项目因缺乏稳定资金和明确领导而失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abundance</td><td>/əˈbʌndəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>an abundance of fresh fruit<br>大量新鲜水果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market offered an abundance of fresh fruit at low prices.  市场以低价供应大量新鲜水果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adequacy</td><td>/ˈædəkwəsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>充足，适当</td><td>the adequacy of funding<br>资金是否充足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors questioned the adequacy of the building's fire safety measures.  检查员质疑该建筑消防措施是否充足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amplitude</td><td>/ˈæmpləˌtuːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>充裕，广大</td><td>amplitude of resources<br>资源充裕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library's amplitude of rare manuscripts attracts scholars from many countries.  该图书馆丰富的珍稀手稿吸引了许多国家的学者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 opulence</td><td>/ˈɑːpjələns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>富裕，丰裕</td><td>live in sheer opulence<br>生活极度奢华</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The palace displayed an opulence that stunned every visiting guest.  这座宫殿展现出令每位来客震惊的奢华。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plenitude</td><td>/ˈplɛnɪˌtud/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>充足，大量</td><td>a plenitude of evidence<br>充足的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The archive offered a plenitude of letters and diaries.  这个档案馆提供了大量书信和日记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sufficiency</td><td>/səˈfɪʃənsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>充足，足量</td><td>a sufficiency of food<br>食物充足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The harvest guaranteed a sufficiency of grain for the entire winter.  这次收成保证了整个冬天粮食充足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wealth</td><td>/ˈwelθ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，丰富；财富</td><td>a wealth of experience<br>丰富的经验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old editor brought a wealth of experience to the team.  这位老编辑给团队带来了丰富的经验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>province</b></td><td>/ˈprɑːvɪns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>职能范围</td><td>outside my province<br>超出我的职责范围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Auditing the accounts falls outside my province as a technical adviser.  作为技术顾问，审计账目超出了我的职能范围。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 business</td><td>/ˈbɪznəs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>本分，职责；事务</td><td>none of your business<br>不关你的事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Judging private morals is not the court's business.  评判私人道德不是法庭的职责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 duty</td><td>/ˈduːti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>职责，本分</td><td>in the line of duty<br>在履行职责时</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Checking the equipment was clearly the engineer's duty.  检查设备显然是那位工程师的职责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 function</td><td>/ˈfʌŋkʃən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>职能，作用</td><td>the proper function<br>应有的职能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proper function of a review board is to advise.  评审委员会的应有职能是提供建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 role</td><td>/ˈroʊl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>职责，角色</td><td>play a supporting role<br>扮演辅助角色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her role was to coordinate the volunteers, not command them.  她的职责是协调志愿者，而非指挥他们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>peck</b></td><td>/pek/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>小口啄食，小口地吃</td><td>peck at the food<br>小口啄食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nervous child only pecked at her dinner and left the table early.  紧张的孩子只小口吃了几下晚饭就早早离席。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abundance</td><td>/əˈbʌndəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>an abundance of fresh fruit<br>大量新鲜水果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market offered an abundance of fresh fruit at low prices.  市场以低价供应大量新鲜水果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bunch</td><td>/ˈbʌntʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一群，一束，一串</td><td>a bunch of keys<br>一串钥匙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bunch of students waited outside the lecture hall.  一群学生在讲堂外等着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bundle</td><td>/ˈbʌndəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>匆忙送走，急送</td><td>bundle him into a car<br>把他匆匆塞进车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guards bundled the prisoner into a waiting van.  卫兵把犯人匆匆塞进等候的货车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dozen</td><td>/ˈdʌzən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>许多，一打</td><td>dozens of complaints<br>数十起投诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dozens of complaints arrived within the first week.  第一周内就来了数十起投诉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mass</td><td>/ˈmæs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，大批</td><td>a mass of documents<br>一大堆文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A mass of documents buried the clerk's small desk.  一大堆文件埋住了那位职员的小书桌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mountain</td><td>/ˈmaʊntən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，堆积如山</td><td>a mountain of paperwork<br>堆积如山的文书</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced a mountain of paperwork after the audit.  审计之后她面对堆积如山的文书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 much</td><td>/ˈmʌtʃ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词、副词</td><td>许多，大量</td><td>not much money left<br>剩下的钱不多</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was not much money left after the repairs.  修理之后剩下的钱不多了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 multiplicity</td><td>/ˌmʌltəˈplɪsəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>多样性，多重性</td><td>a multiplicity of causes<br>多重原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The failure resulted from a multiplicity of small errors.  这次失败源于多种小错误的叠加。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 myriad</td><td>/ˈmɪriəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无限的，大量的</td><td>myriad possibilities<br>无数的可能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new technology opens myriad possibilities for teaching students in remote villages.  这项新技术为教偏远村庄的学生打开了无数可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pile</td><td>/ˈpaɪl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>堆积，累积；一堆</td><td>pile up quickly<br>迅速堆积起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unread messages piled up while I was away on holiday.  我休假期间未读消息堆积起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plenitude</td><td>/ˈplɛnɪˌtud/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>充足，大量</td><td>a plenitude of evidence<br>充足的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The archive offered a plenitude of letters and diaries.  这个档案馆提供了大量书信和日记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profusion</td><td>/prəˈfjuːʒn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>a profusion of flowers<br>繁花盛开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A profusion of wildflowers covered the hillside after the spring rains.  春雨过后，山坡上开满了大量野花。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ton</td><td>/ˈtʌn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，很多；吨</td><td>tons of homework<br>大量的作业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children came home with tons of homework.  孩子们带着大量作业回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 volume</td><td>/ˈvɑːljum/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量；容量；卷</td><td>a great volume of traffic<br>巨大的车流量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge cannot handle this volume of traffic.  这座桥承受不了如此大的车流量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wealth</td><td>/ˈwelθ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大量，丰富；财富</td><td>a wealth of experience<br>丰富的经验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old editor brought a wealth of experience to the team.  这位老编辑给团队带来了丰富的经验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bite</td><td>/ˈbaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>咬；一口</td><td>take a bite<br>咬一口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took only one small bite and pushed the plate away.  她只咬了一小口就把盘子推开了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nibble</td><td>/ˈnɪbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>小口咬</td><td>nibble at the cheese<br>小口啃干酪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mouse nibbled at the cheese we had left beside the kitchen door.  老鼠小口啃着我们放在厨房门边的干酪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sip</td><td>/ˈsɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>小口喝，抿</td><td>sip hot tea slowly<br>慢慢抿热茶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sipped her tea and watched the rain through the window.  她抿着茶，透过窗子看雨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tipple</td><td>/ˈtɪpəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>常喝酒，小酌；酒</td><td>tipple in the evening<br>傍晚小酌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old sailor tippled quietly by the fire every evening.  这位老水手每晚都在火炉边静静小酌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bit</td><td>/ˈbɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一点，少量</td><td>a bit of luck<br>一点运气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Add just a bit of salt to the simmering sauce.  往煮着的酱汁里加一点点盐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glimmer</td><td>/ˈɡlɪmər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，一丝；微光</td><td>a glimmer of hope<br>一丝希望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letter offered a faint glimmer of hope.  那封信带来一丝微弱的希望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 handful</td><td>/ˈhændˌfʊl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>少数，一把</td><td>only a handful of guests<br>只有少数客人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a handful of guests stayed until the end.  只有少数客人留到最后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hint</td><td>/ˈhɪnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暗示，提示</td><td>drop a hint<br>透露一点暗示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave me a subtle hint about the surprise party.  她微妙地暗示了那个惊喜派对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 little</td><td>/ˈlɪtəl/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微小的，少的</td><td>a little detail<br>一个小细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She noticed one little detail that everyone else had missed.  她注意到一个别人都漏掉的小细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mite</td><td>/maɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>微小的东西，很少的钱</td><td>a mite of comfort<br>一点点安慰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brief letter offered a mite of comfort during those difficult winter months.  他简短的信在那些艰难的冬月里带来了一点点安慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nip</td><td>/nɪp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小口吃喝</td><td>a nip of whisky<br>一小口威士忌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He poured himself a nip of whisky and sat down by the fire.  他给自己倒了一小口威士忌，坐到火边。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ounce</td><td>/ˈaʊns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量，一点；盎司</td><td>every ounce of strength<br>每一分力气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used every ounce of strength to lift the beam.  他用尽每一分力气抬起那根梁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peanuts</td><td>/ˈpiːnʌts/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极少的钱，微薄之数</td><td>work for peanuts<br>为微薄报酬工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young interns worked for peanuts and long hours.  年轻实习生拿着微薄报酬长时间工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pittance</td><td>/ˈpɪtns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>少量津贴</td><td>work for a pittance<br>为微薄报酬工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The apprentices worked long hours for a pittance and no benefits.  学徒们长时间工作，只拿微薄津贴且没有福利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spot</td><td>/ˈspɑːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>污点，斑点；地点</td><td>a spot on the shirt<br>衬衫上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A grease spot on the sleeve ruined his only white shirt.  袖子上的油污弄坏了他唯一的白衬衫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sprinkle</td><td>/ˈsprɪŋkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>少量，星星点点</td><td>a sprinkle of sugar<br>少量的糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cook added only a sprinkle of sugar to the batter.  厨师只往面糊里加了少量的糖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trace</td><td>/ˈtreɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>痕迹；微量</td><td>a trace of irony<br>一丝讽意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tea showed only a trace of bitterness.  这茶只有一丝苦味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 devour</td><td>/dɪˈvaʊər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>吞食，耗尽</td><td>devour the entire meal<br>吞掉整顿饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hungry children devoured the entire meal in five minutes.  饥饿的孩子们五分钟内吞掉了整顿饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gobble</td><td>/ˈɡɑːbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>狼吞虎咽</td><td>gobble down the meal<br>狼吞虎咽吃完饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children gobbled down their sandwiches and ran outside to play again.  孩子们狼吞虎咽吃完三明治，又跑到外面去玩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gorge</td><td>/ɡɔːrdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>峡谷；狼吞虎咽</td><td>gorge oneself on cake<br>大吃蛋糕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We gorged ourselves on fresh mangoes until nobody could eat another bite.  我们大吃新鲜芒果，直到谁也再吃不下一口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 guzzle</td><td>/ˈɡʌzl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>狂饮</td><td>guzzle a can of beer<br>狂饮一罐啤酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He guzzled two bottles of soda before the game even started.  比赛还没开始他就狂饮了两瓶汽水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quaff</td><td>/kwæf/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛饮；大口喝</td><td>quaff a mug of ale<br>大口喝下一杯麦酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thirsty travelers quaffed mugs of ale at the roadside inn.  口渴的旅人在路边客栈痛饮大杯麦酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 swill</td><td>/ˈswɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>痛饮，大口喝；冲洗</td><td>swill down a beer<br>痛饮一杯啤酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They swilled cheap beer all night in the noisy bar.  他们在嘈杂的酒吧里痛饮廉价啤酒一整夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pedagogue</b></td><td>/ˈpedəɡɑːɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>教育者，老师</td><td>a strict old pedagogue<br>一位严厉的老教师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A strict old pedagogue drilled Latin verbs into us every morning.  一位严厉的老教师每天早上让我们反复操练拉丁语动词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 educator</td><td>/ˈedʒəkeɪtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>教育者，教师</td><td>a respected educator<br>受人敬重的教育者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became the most respected educator in the district.  她成了本区最受敬重的教育者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 instructor</td><td>/ɪnˈstrʌktər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>教员，指导者</td><td>a driving instructor<br>驾驶教练</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our swimming instructor made every lesson feel easy.  我们的游泳教练让每节课都轻松起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preceptor</td><td>/prɪˈsɛptər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>导师，教师</td><td>his lifelong preceptor<br>他终身的导师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young monk revered his preceptor above all others.  这位小僧最敬重他的导师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 teacher</td><td>/ˈtiːtʃər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>教师，老师</td><td>a gifted teacher<br>极有天赋的老师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A gifted teacher can change a child's whole future.  一位有天赋的老师能改变孩子的一生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disciple</td><td>/dɪˈsaɪpl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>信徒，门徒，追随者</td><td>a devoted disciple<br>忠诚的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became a devoted disciple of the old master painter.  他成了这位老画家的忠实追随者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pupil</td><td>/ˈpjuːpəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>学生，弟子</td><td>a promising pupil<br>很有前途的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was her most promising pupil that difficult year.  在那个艰难的年份他是她最有前途的学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 student</td><td>/ˈstuːdənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>学生，学习者</td><td>a diligent student<br>勤奋的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every student in the class read the difficult text.  班上每个学生都读了那篇难懂的文章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pedant</b></td><td>/ˈpedənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>书呆子，墨守成规之人</td><td>a fussy old pedant<br>一个爱挑剔的老书呆子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a pedant would object to ending a sentence with a preposition.  只有墨守成规之人才会反对句子以介词结尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doctrinaire</td><td>/ˌdɑːktrəˈner/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>教条主义的，照本宣科的</td><td>a doctrinaire approach<br>教条主义的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His doctrinaire approach left no room for evidence that contradicted the theory.  他教条主义的做法容不下任何与理论相悖的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dogmatist</del></td><td>/ˈdɔːɡmətɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>固执己见者，教条主义者</td><td>an inflexible dogmatist<br>顽固的教条主义者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor argued like a dogmatist, ignoring every inconvenient fact.  这位教授像教条主义者一样争辩，无视一切不利事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>predestine</b></del></td><td>/ˌpriːˈdestɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先注定</td><td>predestine his failure<br>注定他的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old prophecy seemed to predestine the prince to an early death.  那个古老的预言似乎注定了王子会早逝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doom</td><td>/duːm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使注定（失败或毁灭）</td><td>doom the project to failure<br>使项目注定失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A shortage of funds doomed the project to failure before it began.  资金短缺使这个项目在开始之前就注定失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fate</td><td>/ˈfeɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>命运，宿命；注定</td><td>seal someone's fate<br>决定某人的命运</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark sealed his political fate.  一句不慎的话决定了他的政治命运。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 foredoom</del></td><td>/fɔrˈdum/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先注定（失败）</td><td>foredoom the plan<br>使计划注定失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poor funding foredoomed the expedition from the start.  资金不足从一开始就注定了这次探险的失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 foreordain</del></td><td>/ˌfɔrɔrˈdeɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先注定，天命预定</td><td>foreordain the outcome<br>预先注定结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The theologians argued that God foreordains every event.  神学家们主张上帝预先注定每一件事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ordain</td><td>/ɔːrˈdeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>命令，规定；任命神职</td><td>ordain that all must attend<br>规定所有人必须出席</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old charter ordained that meetings be held every spring.  旧宪章规定会议须于每年春季举行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predetermine</td><td>/ˌpriːdɪˈtɜːrmən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先决定，预先确定</td><td>predetermine the result<br>预先决定结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Genes do not entirely predetermine a person's health.  基因并不完全预先决定一个人的健康。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 preordain</del></td><td>/ˌpriːɔːrˈdeɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先注定，预定</td><td>seem preordained to fail<br>似乎注定要失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The outcome seemed preordained long before the vote.  结果在投票很久之前似乎就已注定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pedestrian</b></td><td>/pəˈdestriən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平庸无奇的，令人厌倦的</td><td>a pedestrian style<br>平庸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reviewer called the plot pedestrian and the dialogue painfully flat.  评论者称其情节平庸，对话乏味得令人难受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arid</td><td>/ˈærɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>枯燥无趣的；干燥的</td><td>an arid lecture<br>枯燥的讲座</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor's arid lecture emptied half the seats before the break.  这位教授枯燥乏味的讲座在课间前就空了一半座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreary</td><td>/ˈdrɪri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调乏味的</td><td>a dreary afternoon<br>沉闷的下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a dreary afternoon indoors watching rain streak the windows.  我们在室内度过一个沉闷的下午，看着雨水划过窗玻璃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flat</td><td>/ˈflæt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调无光的，乏味的</td><td>a flat performance<br>平淡无味的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paint dried to a flat, lifeless gray.  油漆干成了一种无光而死板的灰色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 Jading</del></td><td>/ˈdʒeɪdɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌倦的，使人生厌</td><td>a jading routine<br>令人厌倦的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of jading paperwork dulled his early enthusiasm.  多年令人厌倦的文书工作磨掉了他早年的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jejune</td><td>/dʒɪˈdʒuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣乏味的</td><td>a jejune argument<br>乏味浅薄的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics dismissed the sequel as jejune and repetitive.  评论家认为这部续集乏味且重复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monochromatic</td><td>/ˌmɑːnəkroʊˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单色的</td><td>a monochromatic palette<br>单色的配色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The designer chose a monochromatic scheme of soft grays.  设计师选了柔灰的单色方案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monotonous</td><td>/məˈnɑːtənəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无变化的</td><td>a monotonous voice<br>单调的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His monotonous voice made the afternoon lecture almost impossible to sit through.  他单调的声音让下午的讲座几乎难以听完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stale</td><td>/steɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧乏味的</td><td>stale jokes<br>老掉牙的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stale jokes made the audience shift restlessly in their seats.  他老掉牙的笑话让观众在座位上不安地挪动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stodgy</td><td>/ˈstɑːdʒi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古板守旧的，乏味的</td><td>a stodgy old textbook<br>一本乏味的旧教材</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The course relied on a stodgy old textbook nobody enjoyed reading.  这门课用的是一本没人爱读的乏味旧教材。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tedious</td><td>/ˈtiːdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长乏味的</td><td>a tedious explanation<br>冗长乏味的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager gave a tedious explanation of the new filing system.  经理对新的归档系统作了冗长乏味的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wearisome</td><td>/ˈwɪrisəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌倦的，乏味的</td><td>a wearisome journey<br>令人疲乏的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wearisome journey took three days by slow train.  这段疲乏的旅程坐慢车要三天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absorbing</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrbɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，吸引人的</td><td>an absorbing novel<br>引人入胜的小说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found her account of the voyage utterly absorbing.  我觉得她对航行的叙述极其引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engrossing</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，令人专注的</td><td>an engrossing story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary was so engrossing that nobody left early.  这部纪录片如此引人入胜，没人提早离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gripping</td><td>/ˈɡrɪpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扣人心弦的，紧张的</td><td>a gripping thriller<br>扣人心弦的惊悚片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final chapter is gripping from start to finish.  最后一章从头到尾都扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interesting</td><td>/ˈɪntrəstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有趣的，引人入胜的</td><td>an interesting proposal<br>一个有趣的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an interesting objection nobody had anticipated.  她提出了一个无人预料到的有趣反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intriguing</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtriːɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人好奇的，有趣的</td><td>an intriguing question<br>引人好奇的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an intriguing point that nobody had considered.  她提出了一个无人考虑过的有趣观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 involving</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlvɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，扣人心弦的</td><td>an involving story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary is more involving than any thriller this year.  这部纪录片比今年任何惊悚片都更扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>peep</b></td><td>/piːp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一瞥</td><td>take a quick peep<br>快速瞥一眼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took a quick peep at the answers before the teacher returned.  老师回来之前，她飞快地瞥了一眼答案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gander</td><td>/ˈɡændər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一瞥，看一眼；雄鹅</td><td>take a gander at this<br>瞧一眼这个</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Take a gander at the new engine before we start.  开工之前先瞧一眼新引擎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glance</td><td>/ˈɡlæns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>扫视，一瞥</td><td>glance at his watch<br>看一眼手表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She glanced at her watch and hurried toward the front door.  她看了一眼手表，匆匆走向前门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glimpse</td><td>/ˈɡlɪmps/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>一瞥，瞥见</td><td>catch a glimpse of her<br>瞥见她一眼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I caught only a glimpse of the singer through the crowd.  我只在人群中瞥见了歌手一眼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peek</td><td>/ˈpiːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>偷看，一瞥</td><td>sneak a peek<br>偷瞄一眼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child sneaked a peek at the wrapped presents.  孩子偷瞄了一眼包好的礼物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>peer</b></td><td>/pɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>同等地位的人，同辈；好奇地凝视</td><td>respected by his peers<br>受同辈尊重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His work is widely respected by his peers in the field.  他的研究在业内同辈中广受尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coequal</td><td>/ˌkoʊˈiːkwəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>同等的，地位平等的</td><td>coequal branches of government<br>地位平等的政府分支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The constitution treats the three branches as coequal partners in governing.  宪法把三个分支视为地位平等的治理伙伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 compeer</del></td><td>/kəmˈpɪr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>同辈，地位相当者</td><td>without a compeer<br>无人与之匹敌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a violinist she had no compeer in her generation.  作为小提琴家，她在同辈中无人匹敌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coordinate</td><td>/koʊˈɔːrdɪneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>协调，使配合</td><td>coordinate the relief effort<br>协调救援工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two agencies coordinated the rescue effort after the flood.  洪水过后两个机构协调了救援工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 counterpart</td><td>/ˈkaʊntərpɑːrt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（地位、功能）对等的人或物</td><td>his French counterpart<br>他的法国对等人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our finance minister met his German counterpart to discuss the trade dispute.  我们的财政部长会见德国的对等官员，商讨贸易争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivalent</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvələnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>等价的，相等的</td><td>the equivalent amount<br>等量的数额</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered her the equivalent amount in shares instead of cash.  他们提出用等量的股份代替现金付给她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fellow</td><td>/ˈfeloʊ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>同伴，同辈；家伙</td><td>his fellow researchers<br>他的研究同行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fellow researchers doubted the result at first.  他的研究同行起初怀疑这个结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 match</td><td>/ˈmætʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>与…相配，比得上</td><td>match his rival's skill<br>比得上对手的技艺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few players could match his skill on wet grass.  湿草地上少有球员比得上他的技艺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 parallel</td><td>/ˈperəˌlel/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相似的，对应的；平行的</td><td>a parallel case<br>类似的案例</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Historians found a parallel case in a much earlier war.  历史学家在一场更早的战争中找到了类似的例子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blink</td><td>/ˈblɪŋk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>眨眼；闪烁</td><td>blink in surprise<br>惊讶地眨眼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She blinked several times before her eyes adjusted to the light.  她眨了几下眼才适应光线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gape</td><td>/ˈɡeɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>目瞪口呆地看，张口凝视</td><td>gape in astonishment<br>惊讶地张口凝视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourists gaped at the collapsed tower for hours.  游客们盯着倒塌的塔看了好几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gawk</td><td>/ˈɡɔːk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>呆呆地看，傻看</td><td>gawk at the accident<br>呆看事故现场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourists stood gawking at the enormous new glass tower.  游客们站着呆看那座巨大的新玻璃塔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gaze</td><td>/ˈɡeɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>凝视，注视</td><td>gaze at the stars<br>凝望星空</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They gazed at the mountains until the sun had finally set.  他们凝望群山，直到太阳终于落下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 goggle</td><td>/ˈɡɑːɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>凝视</td><td>goggle at the screen<br>凝视屏幕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tourists goggled at the enormous statue for several silent minutes.  游客们默默凝视那尊巨大的雕像好几分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rubberneck</td><td>/ˈrʌbərˌnɛk/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>伸颈张望，好奇围观</td><td>rubberneck at the crash<br>伸颈围观车祸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drivers slowed to rubberneck at the overturned truck.  司机们减速伸头围观翻倒的卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stare</td><td>/ˈster/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>盯着看，凝视</td><td>stare in disbelief<br>难以置信地盯着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stared at the letter for a long time without speaking.  他久久盯着那封信，一言不发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inferior</td><td>/ɪnˈfɪriər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>低等的，次等的</td><td>inferior in quality<br>质量较差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Customers complained that the new fabric was clearly inferior.  顾客抱怨新面料明显质量较差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glance</td><td>/ˈɡlæns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>扫视，一瞥</td><td>glance at his watch<br>看一眼手表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She glanced at her watch and hurried toward the front door.  她看了一眼手表，匆匆走向前门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glimpse</td><td>/ˈɡlɪmps/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>一瞥，瞥见</td><td>catch a glimpse of her<br>瞥见她一眼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I caught only a glimpse of the singer through the crowd.  我只在人群中瞥见了歌手一眼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>peeve</b></td><td>/piːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，惹恼</td><td>peeve the audience<br>惹恼观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud phone conversations on the train really peeve the other passengers.  火车上大声打电话确实惹恼其他乘客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annoy</td><td>/əˈnɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，不断烦扰</td><td>annoy the neighbors<br>不断打扰邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The barking dog began to annoy every neighbor on the quiet street.  那只不停犬吠的狗开始烦扰这条安静街上的每位邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bother</td><td>/ˈbɑːðər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>打扰，使烦恼；费心</td><td>don't bother me<br>别烦我</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The constant noise from the street really bothers me.  街上不停的噪音真让我烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exasperate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæspəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，激怒</td><td>exasperate his parents<br>使他的父母恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His constant lateness exasperated even the most patient teachers.  他总是迟到，连最有耐心的老师都被激怒了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frost</td><td>/ˈfrɔːst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使恼怒；冷淡对待</td><td>frost the whole audience<br>把全场观众惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug reply frosted everyone in the small room.  他自得的回答把小屋里的人都惹恼了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gall</td><td>/ɡɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（使）焦躁，激怒</td><td>galled by the remark<br>被那句话惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug tone galled everyone sitting around the conference table.  他自鸣得意的语气激怒了围坐在会议桌旁的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irk</td><td>/ɜːrk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使厌倦</td><td>irk his colleagues<br>使同事厌烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His habit of humming while working irked everyone in the office.  他工作时哼歌的习惯使办公室里所有人都很厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nettle</td><td>/ˈnetl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>惹怒</td><td>nettled by criticism<br>被批评惹怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was clearly nettled by the reporter's rude question.  记者无礼的提问显然惹恼了她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pique</td><td>/piːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起（兴趣）；使生气</td><td>pique her curiosity<br>激起她的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange title piqued her curiosity, so she borrowed the book.  奇怪的书名激起了她的好奇心，于是她借走了那本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rile</td><td>/raɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，惹怒</td><td>rile up the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing riles him more than being interrupted in the middle of a sentence.  没有什么比话说到一半被打断更让他恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruffle</td><td>/ˈrʌfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>弄乱（毛发）；扰乱，使恼怒</td><td>ruffle his feathers<br>惹他不快</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single rude question was enough to ruffle the normally calm witness.  一个无礼的问题就足以扰乱这位平素冷静的证人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vex</td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 becalm</del></td><td>/bɪˈkɑːm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，使停滞不动</td><td>becalmed by light winds<br>因风力太小而停滞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The yacht lay becalmed for two days without any breeze.  游艇因无风停滞了两天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compose</td><td>/kəmˈpoʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使镇定；组成；创作</td><td>compose oneself<br>让自己镇定下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a breath to compose himself before speaking.  他深吸一口气让自己镇定下来才开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lull</td><td>/lʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>相对平静时期，间隙；使镇静，使安心</td><td>a lull in the fighting<br>战斗中的间隙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief lull in the fighting allowed medics to reach the wounded.  战斗中短暂的间隙让医护人员得以接近伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lullaby</td><td>/ˈlʌləbaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>摇篮曲；唱摇篮曲使入睡</td><td>sing a gentle lullaby<br>唱一首轻柔的摇篮曲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sang a gentle lullaby until the restless baby finally fell asleep.  她唱着轻柔的摇篮曲，直到不安的婴儿终于睡着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiet</td><td>/ˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安静的；平静的</td><td>keep the room quiet<br>保持房间安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library stays quiet even during exam week.  即使在考试周，图书馆也保持安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 salve</td><td>/sæv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>减轻，缓解</td><td>salve one's conscience<br>减轻内心的不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave money to the school to salve his uneasy conscience.  他向学校捐钱，以减轻内心的不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 settle</td><td>/ˈsetəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>解决，平息；定居</td><td>settle the dispute<br>解决争端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides finally settled their dispute out of court.  双方最终在庭外解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 still</td><td>/ˈstɪl/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>寂静无声的；寂静</td><td>the still of night<br>夜晚的寂静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The air was still and the lake looked like glass.  空气静止无风，湖面像玻璃一样。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tranquilize</td><td>/ˈtræŋkwəˌlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>使平静，给…服镇静剂</td><td>tranquilize the animal<br>给动物注射镇静剂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vet had to tranquilize the frightened horse.  兽医不得不给这匹惊慌的马注射镇静剂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pejorative</b></td><td>/pɪˈdʒɔːrətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻蔑的，贬低的</td><td>a pejorative label<br>带贬义的标签</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics used the word in a purely pejorative sense.  评论家用这个词纯粹是取其贬义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belittling</td><td>/bɪˈlɪtəlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻蔑的，贬低的</td><td>belittling comments<br>贬低人的评语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her belittling comments discouraged the younger staff.  她贬低人的评语让年轻员工失去信心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contemptuous</td><td>/kənˈtemptʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻蔑的，鄙视的</td><td>a contemptuous glance<br>轻蔑的一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave the proposal a contemptuous glance and left.  他轻蔑地看了一眼提案就走了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degrading</td><td>/dɪˈɡreɪdɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有损尊严的，侮辱性的</td><td>degrading treatment<br>有损尊严的对待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Prisoners complained of degrading treatment by the guards.  囚犯抱怨受到看守有损尊严的对待。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 deprecatory</del></td><td>/ˈdɛprɪkəˌtɔri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贬低的，不赞成的</td><td>a deprecatory tone<br>贬抑的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke of her own work in a deprecatory tone.  她用贬抑的语气谈论自己的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 depreciative</del></td><td>/dɪˈpriʃiətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻视的，贬损的</td><td>depreciative remarks<br>贬损的言辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His depreciative remarks about rivals damaged his reputation.  他贬损对手的言辞损害了自己的声誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 derogative</del></td><td>/dɪˈrɑɡətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贬义的，贬损的</td><td>a derogative nickname<br>带贬义的绰号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paper dropped that derogative nickname after complaints.  在收到投诉后，报纸弃用了那个贬义绰号。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disdainful</td><td>/dɪsˈdeɪnfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻蔑的，不屑的</td><td>disdainful silence<br>不屑的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He met the accusation with disdainful silence.  他以不屑的沉默回应指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disparaging</td><td>/dɪˈsperɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贬低的，轻蔑的</td><td>disparaging remarks<br>贬低的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Disparaging remarks about volunteers upset the staff.  贬低志愿者的言论让员工不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scornful</td><td>/ˈskɔːrnfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻蔑的，嘲讽的</td><td>a scornful laugh<br>轻蔑的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A scornful laugh greeted the young man's suggestion.  年轻人的建议招来一声轻蔑的笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commendatory</td><td>/kəˈmendətɔːri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>赞许的，表扬的</td><td>a commendatory letter<br>一封表扬信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His file contains three commendatory letters from clients.  他的档案里有三封客户的表扬信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 complimentary</td><td>/ˌkɑːmpləˈmentəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>免费赠送的；赞美的</td><td>a complimentary breakfast<br>免费的早餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel offers a complimentary breakfast to every overnight guest.  酒店为每位住宿客人提供免费早餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laudatory</td><td>/ˈlɔːdətɔːri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表示赞扬的</td><td>laudatory reviews<br>赞扬性的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel received laudatory reviews from critics who rarely praise debut authors.  这部小说获得了盛赞，而这些评论家很少称赞新人作家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pellucid</b></td><td>/pəˈluːsɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰明确的，易懂的</td><td>pellucid prose<br>明白易懂的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pellucid explanation made the theory seem obvious.  他清晰的讲解让这个理论显得浅显易懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apparent</td><td>/əˈperənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的；表面上的</td><td>for no apparent reason<br>没有明显原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It soon became apparent that the machine had never worked.  很快就明显看出这台机器从未运转过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinct</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰的，明显的</td><td>a distinct impression<br>清晰的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a distinct difference between the two dialects.  这两种方言之间有明显差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evident</td><td>/ˈevɪdənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显然的</td><td>clearly evident<br>显而易见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her talent for music was evident from a very early age.  她的音乐才华从很小就显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lucid</td><td>/ˈluːsɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达清晰的，易懂的</td><td>a lucid explanation<br>清晰的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor offered a lucid summary of a difficult argument.  教授对一个艰深论证做了清晰的总结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 manifest</td><td>/ˈmænɪfest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>显然的，明显易懂的；显现，显露</td><td>manifest injustice<br>明显的不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His discomfort at the question was manifest to everyone seated around the table.  他对这个问题的不适让围坐桌边的每个人都看得明明白白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obvious</td><td>/ˈɑːbviəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显而易见的</td><td>an obvious mistake<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The answer seemed obvious once she explained the method.  她解释了方法后，答案似乎显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 palpable</td><td>/ˈpælpəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，易觉察的</td><td>a palpable sense of relief<br>明显的宽慰感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A palpable tension filled the room as the verdict was read aloud.  宣读判决时，房间里弥漫着明显的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 patent</td><td>/ˈpeɪtnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>显而易见的，明显的；专利</td><td>a patent lie<br>显而易见的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The excuse was a patent lie and everyone knew it.  这个借口是显而易见的谎言，人人都知道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 perspicuous</del></td><td>/pərˈspɪkjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表述清晰的，明白易懂的</td><td>a perspicuous style<br>明白易懂的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His writing is perspicuous even when the topic is technical.  即使话题很专业，他的文章也清晰易懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 straightforward</td><td>/ˌstreɪtˈfɔːrwərd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单明了的；坦率的</td><td>a straightforward procedure<br>简单直接的流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The instructions are straightforward enough for a child to follow.  说明简单明了，连孩子都能照着做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clouded</td><td>/ˈklaʊdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浑浊的，模糊不清的</td><td>clouded water<br>浑浊的水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rain left the pond clouded for several days.  雨水使池塘浑浊了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cryptic</td><td>/ˈkrɪptɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秘密的；难以理解的</td><td>a cryptic remark<br>费解的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His cryptic note gave us no hint about where he had gone.  他那张费解的便条没有透露他去了哪里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enigmatic</td><td>/ˌenɪɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>an enigmatic smile<br>神秘的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enigmatic answer left the reporters guessing for several days.  她神秘的回答让记者们猜了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indistinct</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊不清的，含糊的</td><td>an indistinct outline<br>模糊的轮廓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph shows an indistinct figure by the door.  照片上门边有个模糊的人影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mysterious</td><td>/mɪˈstɪriəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>a mysterious stranger<br>神秘的陌生人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A mysterious light appeared over the lake around midnight.  午夜时分湖面上出现了一道神秘的光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obfuscated</td><td>/ˈɑbfəskeɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混淆不清的，弄糊涂的</td><td>obfuscated legal language<br>晦涩的法律语言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was so obfuscated that nobody could find the real numbers.  这份报告写得如此含混，没人能找到真实数据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>plaintive</b></td><td>/ˈpleɪntɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>哀伤的，悲切的</td><td>a plaintive melody<br>哀伤的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A plaintive melody drifted from the open window of the old house.  一段哀伤的旋律从那座老房子敞开的窗子里飘出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aching</td><td>/ˈeɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>痛苦的，酸痛的</td><td>an aching heart<br>痛苦的心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote the letter with an aching heart.  她怀着痛苦的心写下那封信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 anguished</td><td>/ˈæŋɡwɪʃt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极度痛苦的</td><td>an anguished cry<br>痛苦的哭喊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An anguished cry came from the far end of the corridor.  走廊尽头传来一声痛苦的哭喊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bemoaning</td><td>/bɪˈmoʊnɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>哀叹的，抱怨的</td><td>bemoaning his bad luck<br>哀叹自己的坏运气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the evening bemoaning the loss of his job.  他整晚都在哀叹失去工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deploring</td><td>/dɪˈplɔːrɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>哀叹的，痛惜的</td><td>a deploring tone of voice<br>哀叹的语调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spoke of the ruined city in a deploring tone.  他用哀叹的语调谈起那座毁掉的城市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doleful</td><td>/ˈdoʊlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲哀的，忧郁的</td><td>a doleful voice<br>悲哀的语调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told the whole story in a low doleful voice.  她用低沉悲哀的语调讲完了整件事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grieving</td><td>/ˈɡriːvɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的，哀痛的</td><td>a grieving family<br>悲痛的家人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors brought food to the grieving family each evening.  邻居每晚给悲痛的家人送饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heartbroken</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtˌbroʊkən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极度悲伤的，伤心欲绝的</td><td>look utterly heartbroken<br>显得伤心欲绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was heartbroken when the old theater was demolished.  老剧院被拆时她伤心欲绝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lugubrious</td><td>/ləˈɡuːbriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（故作夸张的）悲哀的</td><td>a lugubrious expression<br>夸张悲哀的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wore a lugubrious expression whenever anyone mentioned his ruined garden.  每次有人提到他毁掉的花园，他就露出夸张悲哀的表情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mournful</td><td>/ˈmɔːrnfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的</td><td>a mournful melody<br>悲伤的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A mournful melody drifted from the church as the procession passed by.  送葬队伍经过时，教堂里飘出一段悲伤的旋律。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 plangent</del></td><td>/ˈplændʒənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（声音）响亮而哀伤的</td><td>a plangent cry<br>哀伤的呼声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plangent cry of a gull echoed across the empty beach.  海鸥凄凉的叫声在空荡的海滩上回响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sorrowful</td><td>/ˈsɑːroʊfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的，忧伤的</td><td>a sorrowful farewell<br>悲伤的告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sorrowful eyes told us the news before she spoke.  她悲伤的眼睛在她开口前就告诉了我们消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weeping</td><td>/ˈwiːpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>哭泣的，含泪的</td><td>a weeping crowd<br>哭泣的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A weeping crowd followed the coffin through the square.  哭泣的人群跟着棺木穿过广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 woeful</td><td>/ˈwoʊfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲惨的，糟糕的</td><td>a woeful performance<br>糟糕的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team's woeful defending cost them the championship again.  球队糟糕的防守又一次让他们丢了冠军。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>polymath</b></td><td>/ˈpɑːlimæθ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>学识渊博的人，博学者</td><td>a Renaissance polymath<br>文艺复兴时期的博学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Leonardo was a true polymath, excelling in art and science alike.  达·芬奇是真正的博学者，在艺术和科学上同样出色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pundit</td><td>/ˈpʌndɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>权威人士，专家</td><td>a television pundit<br>电视上的评论专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every pundit predicted a close race and every one was wrong.  每位评论专家都预测选情接近，结果都错了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sage</td><td>/seɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>智者</td><td>an ancient sage<br>古代的智者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers walked for days to ask the old sage for advice.  村民们走了好几天去向那位老智者求教。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savant</td><td>/sæˈvɑːnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>博学之士，学者</td><td>a quiet savant<br>沉静的博学之士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quiet savant spent forty years cataloguing insects in a small museum.  那位沉静的博学之士在小博物馆里花了四十年为昆虫编目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scholar</td><td>/ˈskɑːlər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>学者，博学之人</td><td>a distinguished scholar<br>杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture drew scholars from four different countries.  这场讲座吸引了四个国家的学者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 illiterate</td><td>/ɪˈlɪtərət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>文盲的，不识字的</td><td>functionally illiterate adults<br>实际上文盲的成人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Millions remain illiterate because schools in their villages closed years ago.  数百万人仍是文盲，因为他们村里的学校多年前就关了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>penchant</b></td><td>/ˈpentʃənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>嗜好，强烈爱好</td><td>a penchant for luxury<br>对奢华的偏好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her penchant for dramatic hats made her instantly recognizable.  她对夸张帽子的偏爱让人一眼就认出她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affection</td><td>/əˈfekʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喜爱，钟爱；感情</td><td>an affection for old books<br>对旧书的钟爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He kept a lifelong affection for cheap detective novels.  他一生钟爱廉价侦探小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bias</td><td>/ˈbaɪəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>偏见，偏袒</td><td>free from bias<br>没有偏见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers were kept anonymous to reduce bias in judging the papers.  评审匿名以减少论文评判中的偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disposition</td><td>/ˌdɪspəˈzɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>倾向，性情</td><td>a disposition to argue<br>爱争辩的倾向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a natural disposition to question every rule.  她天生有质疑每条规则的倾向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leaning</td><td>/ˈliːnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>倾斜的；倾向</td><td>a leaning tower<br>倾斜的塔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The leaning wall was propped up with heavy timbers.  倾斜的墙用粗木支撑起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partiality</td><td>/ˌpɑːrʃiˈæləti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>偏爱；偏袒</td><td>a partiality for sweets<br>对甜食的偏爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was accused of partiality toward his own students.  教练被指责偏袒自己的学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predilection</td><td>/ˌpredəˈlekʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>偏爱，偏好</td><td>a predilection for jazz<br>对爵士乐的偏爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic's predilection for older films colored every review.  这位评论家对老电影的偏爱影响了他的每篇评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predisposition</td><td>/ˌpriːdɪspəˈzɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>倾向，易感性</td><td>a genetic predisposition to<br>遗传易感性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some patients have a predisposition to high blood pressure.  有些病人有高血压的易感倾向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proclivity</td><td>/prəˈklɪvəti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>癖性，偏好（多指不良倾向）</td><td>criminal proclivities<br>犯罪倾向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His proclivity for gambling eventually ruined the family.  他的赌博癖好最终毁了这个家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 propensity</td><td>/prəˈpensəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>倾向，癖好</td><td>a propensity to exaggerate<br>夸大的倾向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy has a propensity to lose every umbrella he owns.  这男孩总有把伞丢掉的习性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tendency</td><td>/ˈtendənsi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>倾向，趋势</td><td>a tendency to exaggerate<br>夸张的倾向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has a tendency to exaggerate his own part.  他有夸大自己作用的倾向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aversion</td><td>/əˈvɜːrʒən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感</td><td>a strong aversion to<br>对…强烈厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has a lifelong aversion to crowded parties.  他一生都厌恶拥挤的聚会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disfavor</td><td>/dɪsˈfeɪvər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不赞成；失宠</td><td>fall into disfavor<br>失宠，不受待见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old method gradually fell into disfavor among researchers.  这种旧方法逐渐不受研究者青睐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disinclination</td><td>/ˌdɪsˌɪnklɪˈneɪʃn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不愿，无意</td><td>a disinclination to travel<br>不愿出行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His disinclination to speak in public cost him the promotion.  他不愿公开发言使他失去了升职机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dislike</td><td>/dɪsˈlaɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>不喜欢，厌恶</td><td>dislike crowded places<br>讨厌拥挤的地方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dislikes crowded places and avoids the subway during rush hour.  他讨厌拥挤的地方，高峰时段避开地铁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distaste</td><td>/dɪsˈteɪst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感</td><td>a distaste for gossip<br>对闲话的厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her distaste for gossip kept her out of office politics.  她厌恶闲话，因此远离办公室政治。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loathing</td><td>/ˈloʊθɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，憎恶</td><td>a deep loathing of cruelty<br>对残忍的深切憎恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wrote with obvious loathing about the old regime.  他带着明显的憎恶书写旧政权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repugnance</td><td>/rɪˈpʌɡnəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，憎恶</td><td>repugnance at the idea<br>对这想法感到厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She could not hide her repugnance at the smell of the fish market.  她无法掩饰对鱼市气味的厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repulsion</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感；排斥</td><td>a feeling of repulsion<br>一种厌恶感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photographs produced repulsion rather than sympathy.  这些照片引起的是厌恶而非同情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>penitent</b></td><td>/ˈpenɪtənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔过的</td><td>a penitent sinner<br>悔过的罪人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The penitent thief returned the money and apologized to the shopkeeper.  那个悔过的小偷退还了钱，并向店主道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apologetic</td><td>/əˌpɑːləˈdʒetɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>道歉的，愧疚的</td><td>an apologetic smile<br>带着歉意的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sent an apologetic note explaining why she had missed dinner.  她送来一张致歉的便条，解释为何缺席晚餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 compunctious</del></td><td>/kəmˈpʌŋkʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>内疚的，懊悔的</td><td>a compunctious glance<br>内疚的一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave her a compunctious glance and quietly returned the money.  他内疚地看了她一眼，悄悄把钱还了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regretful</td><td>/rɪˈɡretfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>后悔的，遗憾的</td><td>a regretful glance back<br>懊悔的回望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sounded regretful when she declined the invitation.  她拒绝邀请时语气中带着遗憾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remorseful</td><td>/rɪˈmɔːrsfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔恨的，自责的</td><td>look deeply remorseful<br>显得深深悔恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young thief seemed genuinely remorseful in front of the judge.  这个年轻小偷在法官面前显得真心悔恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repentant</td><td>/rɪˈpentənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔过的</td><td>sound repentant<br>听来悔过</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief looked genuinely repentant as he returned the money to its owner.  那名小偷把钱还给主人时显得真心悔过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rueful</td><td>/ˈruːfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔恨的，懊悔的</td><td>a rueful smile<br>懊悔的苦笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He admitted his error with a rueful smile and a shrug.  他带着懊悔的苦笑和一个耸肩承认了错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sorry</td><td>/ˈsɑːri/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>懊悔的，抱歉的</td><td>genuinely sorry for it<br>真心为此懊悔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seemed genuinely sorry for the trouble he caused.  他似乎真心为自己造成的麻烦懊悔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 impenitent</del></td><td>/ɪmˈpenɪtənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不悔悟的，死不认错的</td><td>an impenitent offender<br>毫无悔意的罪犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief remained impenitent even after hearing the victims speak.  即便听了受害者的陈述，窃贼仍毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 remorseless</td><td>/rɪˈmɔːrsləs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无悔意的，冷酷无情的</td><td>remorseless cruelty<br>冷酷无情的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The killer remained remorseless throughout the long public trial.  凶手在漫长的公开审判中始终毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unapologetic</td><td>/ˌʌnəpɑːləˈdʒetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不认错的，毫无悔意的</td><td>unapologetic about the decision<br>对该决定毫无悔意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained unapologetic about the harsh review.  她对那篇苛评始终毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unrepentant</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈpentənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不悔悟的，毫无悔意的</td><td>remain unrepentant<br>仍毫无悔意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even after the verdict he remained completely unrepentant about the fraud.  即使判决之后，他对这场欺诈仍毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pensive</b></td><td>/ˈpensɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉思的，（尤指）哀思的</td><td>a pensive mood<br>沉思忧郁的心境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew pensive as the train passed her childhood village.  火车经过她童年的村子时，她变得沉思忧郁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 broody</td><td>/ˈbruːdi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉思忧郁的，郁郁不乐的</td><td>a broody silence<br>郁郁的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fell into a broody silence after the phone call.  接完电话后他陷入郁郁的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cogitative</del></td><td>/ˈkɑdʒɪˌteɪtɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉思的，好思考的</td><td>a cogitative pause<br>沉思的停顿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cogitative pause followed the difficult question.  那个难题之后是一段沉思的停顿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meditative</td><td>/ˈmedəˌteɪtɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉思的，冥想的</td><td>in a meditative mood<br>处于沉思的心境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The slow music left the audience in a meditative mood.  舒缓的音乐让观众陷入沉思的心境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 melancholy</td><td>/ˈmelənkɑːli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>忧郁，伤感</td><td>a mood of quiet melancholy<br>淡淡的忧郁情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Autumn rain always fills him with a vague melancholy.  秋雨总让他心里泛起一种莫名的忧郁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 musing</td><td>/ˈmjuːzɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉思的，冥想的</td><td>a musing expression<br>沉思的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered with a musing expression and no words.  她带着沉思的神情作答，一言未发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reflective</td><td>/rɪˈflektɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉思的，深思的</td><td>in a reflective mood<br>处于沉思的状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long illness made him quiet and reflective.  长期的病痛使他变得安静而深思。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruminant</td><td>/ˈruːmənənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>沉思的；反刍动物</td><td>a ruminant silence<br>沉思的静默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat in ruminant silence beside the cold fireplace.  他在冷壁炉旁沉思静坐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruminative</td><td>/ˈrumɪnətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉思的，反复思量的</td><td>a ruminative tone<br>沉思的语调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His late essays have a slow, ruminative tone.  他后期的散文有一种缓慢沉思的语调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thoughtful</td><td>/ˈθɔːtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的；体贴的</td><td>a thoughtful measured reply<br>深思熟虑的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a thoughtful reply after considering the question carefully.  她仔细考虑问题后给出深思熟虑的回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mindless</td><td>/ˈmaɪndləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不需用脑的；愚蠢的</td><td>mindless routine work<br>不需用脑的例行工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wanted mindless work that let him rest his brain.  他想做点不用脑的活儿，让大脑休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 thoughtless</td><td>/ˈθɔːtləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欠考虑的，轻率的</td><td>a thoughtless remark<br>轻率的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was thoughtless of him to slam the door at midnight.  他半夜摔门实在是欠考虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unreflective</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈflɛktɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不加思索的，欠思考的</td><td>an unreflective habit<br>不加思索的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unreflective borrowing of foreign models often fails.  不加思索地照搬外国模式常常失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>penury</b></td><td>/ˈpenjəri/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>贫穷；吝啬，节俭</td><td>live in penury<br>生活贫困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The once wealthy family ended its days in complete penury.  这个曾经富裕的家庭最终在彻底的贫困中度日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 destitution</td><td>/ˌdestəˈtuːʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贫困，穷困潦倒</td><td>live in destitution<br>生活在穷困中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painter died in destitution in a rented attic.  这位画家在租来的阁楼里穷困而死。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 impecuniosity</del></td><td>/ˌɪmpɪˌkjuniˈɑsɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>身无分文，缺钱</td><td>years of impecuniosity<br>多年的手头拮据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of impecuniosity taught him to repair everything himself.  多年的拮据教会他事事自己修补。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impoverishment</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːvərɪʃmənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贫困化，贫乏</td><td>the impoverishment of the region<br>该地区的贫困化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 War brought the impoverishment of once wealthy towns.  战争使昔日富庶的城镇陷入贫困。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indigence</td><td>/ˈɪndɪdʒəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贫穷，贫困</td><td>reduced to indigence<br>陷入贫困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Illness reduced the family to sudden indigence.  疾病使这家人骤然陷入贫困。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neediness</td><td>/ˈnidinəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>贫困，匮乏</td><td>the neediness of the village<br>村子的贫困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Charities measured the neediness of each district carefully.  慈善机构仔细衡量了各区的贫困程度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 poorness</del></td><td>/ˈpʊrnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贫穷；贫瘠</td><td>the poorness of the soil<br>土壤的贫瘠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poorness of the soil limited every harvest here.  土壤贫瘠限制了这里的每次收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poverty</td><td>/ˈpɑːvərti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贫穷，贫困</td><td>live in poverty<br>生活贫困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Half the district still lives in serious poverty.  该区有一半人仍生活在严重贫困中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 want</td><td>/ˈwɑːnt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>缺乏，短缺</td><td>for want of evidence<br>因缺乏证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project collapsed for want of steady funding and clear leadership.  项目因缺乏稳定资金和明确领导而失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 closeness</td><td>/ˈkloʊsnɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>吝啬；亲近</td><td>closeness with money<br>花钱上的吝啬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His closeness with money became a family joke.  他花钱吝啬成了家里的笑谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 miserliness</del></td><td>/ˈmaɪzərlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>吝啬，小气</td><td>notorious miserliness<br>出名的吝啬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His miserliness left the heirs a fortune and no memories.  他的吝啬留给继承人一笔财富却没有回忆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 niggardliness</del></td><td>/ˈnɪɡərdlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>吝啬，小气</td><td>notorious niggardliness<br>出名的吝啬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The landlord's niggardliness left every corridor unlit.  房东的吝啬让每条走廊都没有灯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 parsimony</td><td>/ˈpɑːrsəˌmoʊni/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>吝啬，极度节俭</td><td>fiscal parsimony<br>财政上的极度节俭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Decades of parsimony left the museum without a roof fund.  数十年的过度节俭让博物馆没有修屋顶的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 penuriousness</del></td><td>/pəˈnʊriəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>吝啬；极度贫困</td><td>his stubborn penuriousness<br>他固执的吝啬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His penuriousness surprised guests who knew his wealth.  他的吝啬让知道他财富的客人吃惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stinginess</td><td>/ˈstɪndʒinɪs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>吝啬，小气</td><td>shocking stinginess<br>令人吃惊的吝啬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The waiter complained loudly about the large party's shocking stinginess.  服务员大声抱怨那桌大客人惊人的吝啬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 tightfistedness</del></td><td>/ˌtaɪtˈfɪstɪdnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>吝啬，一毛不拔</td><td>legendary tightfistedness<br>传说中的一毛不拔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The owner's tightfistedness kept wages low for years.  老板的吝啬让工资多年偏低。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
