<tr><td><del><b>pertinacious</b></del></td><td>/ˌpɜːrtnˈeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽固的，固执地坚持的</td><td>pertinacious in her demands<br>固执地坚持要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pertinacious reporter kept calling until she got an answer.  这位执着的记者不停打电话，直到得到答复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adamant</td><td>/ˈædəmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，不可动摇的</td><td>adamant about the rules<br>对规则毫不通融</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was adamant that no player would skip practice.  教练坚决要求没有球员可以缺席训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 headstrong</td><td>/ˈhedˌstrɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>任性的，固执的</td><td>a headstrong child<br>任性的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The headstrong young man ignored every warning from his parents.  这个任性的年轻人对父母的每句劝告都置之不理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 implacable</td><td>/ɪmˈplækəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的；无法平息的</td><td>an implacable enemy<br>不可和解的敌人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced an implacable opponent who refused every reasonable compromise.  她面对一个拒绝一切合理妥协的固执对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflexible</td><td>/ˌɪnˈfleksəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不肯变通的，僵化的</td><td>an inflexible rule<br>不容变通的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was inflexible about the submission deadline.  经理在提交截止日期上毫不通融。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intransigent</td><td>/ɪnˈtrænzɪdʒənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不妥协的，固执的</td><td>an intransigent attitude<br>不妥协的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides remained intransigent, so the negotiations collapsed.  双方都毫不妥协，谈判因此破裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 mulish</del></td><td>/ˈmjuːlɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的</td><td>mulish refusal to listen<br>固执地不肯听</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mulish refusal to listen frustrated everyone on the committee.  他固执地拒绝倾听，让委员会所有人都很沮丧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obdurate</td><td>/ˈɑːbdərət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的；冷酷无情的</td><td>remain obdurate<br>仍然固执不改</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The landlord was obdurate and refused to lower the rent.  房东顽固不化，拒绝降低房租。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perverse</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反常固执的，任性的</td><td>take perverse pleasure<br>反常地感到快意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He takes perverse pleasure in arguing against every popular opinion.  他反常地乐于反驳每一个流行观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stubborn</td><td>/ˈstʌbərn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，顽固的</td><td>as stubborn as a mule<br>像骡子一样固执</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My grandfather was too stubborn to see a doctor.  我祖父太固执，不肯去看医生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 willful</td><td>/ˈwɪlfl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，倔强的</td><td>willful disobedience<br>故意的违抗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His willful refusal to ask for help delayed the repair by weeks.  他固执地拒绝求助，使维修推迟了好几周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compliant</td><td>/kəmˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的</td><td>a compliant employee<br>顺从的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were unusually compliant during the long ceremony.  在漫长的仪式上，孩子们出奇地听话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flexible</td><td>/ˈfleksəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>灵活的，可变的；易受影响的</td><td>a flexible schedule<br>灵活的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offers a flexible schedule to parents of young children.  公司为有幼儿的员工提供灵活的日程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pliable</td><td>/ˈplaɪəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的，柔软的；易受影响的，温顺的</td><td>pliable young minds<br>易受影响的年轻心智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young minds are pliable and easily shaped by the adults around them.  年轻的心智易受影响，很容易被身边的成年人塑造。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pliant</td><td>/ˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的；顺从的</td><td>a pliant assistant<br>顺从的助手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wanted a pliant assistant who would never question his decisions.  他想要一个顺从的助手，永远不会质疑他的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 yielding</td><td>/ˈjiːldɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的，易屈服的</td><td>a yielding personality<br>顺从的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her yielding nature made her an easy target for pushy salesmen.  她顺从的性格使她很容易被强势的推销员盯上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pertinent</b></td><td>/ˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，恰当的</td><td>pertinent to the discussion<br>与讨论相关的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only evidence pertinent to the discussion should be presented today.  今天只应提出与讨论相关的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 applicable</td><td>/ˈæplɪkəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用的，可行的；相关的，有关的</td><td>applicable to all students<br>适用于所有学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is applicable to all students regardless of their year.  这条规定适用于所有学生，不论他们是哪个年级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apposite</td><td>/ˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，合适的</td><td>an apposite comment<br>恰当的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made one apposite comment that settled the whole argument.  她一句恰当的评论就平息了整场争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apropos</td><td>/ˌæprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>相关的；关于，有关</td><td>apropos of nothing<br>毫无来由地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Apropos of nothing, he asked whether I had ever been to Rome.  他毫无来由地问我是否去过罗马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 germane</td><td>/dʒɜːrˈmeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关的，适当的</td><td>germane to the discussion<br>与讨论相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her question was germane to the discussion and changed our plan.  她的问题与讨论密切相关，改变了我们的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 material</td><td>/məˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>物质的；重要的；材料</td><td>material comforts<br>物质上的享受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cared little for material wealth and lived very simply.  他不太在意物质财富，生活极为简朴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pointed</td><td>/ˈpɔɪntɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖锐的，一针见血的</td><td>a pointed question<br>尖锐的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She asked a pointed question that the minister could not answer.  她提出了一个部长无法回答的尖锐问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relative</td><td>/ˈrelətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相关的；相对的；亲属</td><td>relative to the topic<br>与该话题相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Comfort is relative and depends largely on what you expect.  舒适是相对的，很大程度上取决于你的期望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relevant</td><td>/ˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关系的，重要的</td><td>relevant to the case<br>与本案有关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the old letters were relevant to the case.  法官裁定那些旧信件与本案有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraneous</td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immaterial</td><td>/ˌɪməˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>immaterial to the case<br>与本案无关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Whether he liked the plan is immaterial to the legal question here.  他是否喜欢这个计划与这里的法律问题无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inapplicable</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈplɪkəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适用的，不相关的</td><td>inapplicable to this case<br>不适用于本案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old rule is inapplicable to digital contracts.  旧规则不适用于电子合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inapposite</del></td><td>/ɪnˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恰当的，不相关的</td><td>an inapposite comparison<br>不恰当的比较</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comparison to wartime rationing seems inapposite today.  把它比作战时配给在今天显得不恰当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irrelevant</td><td>/ˌɪˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>an irrelevant detail<br>无关的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected because the question was completely irrelevant here.  律师提出反对，因为这个问题在此完全无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pointless</td><td>/ˈpɔɪntləs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无意义的，徒劳的</td><td>a pointless argument<br>毫无意义的争论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arguing with him about politics is completely pointless and exhausting.  和他争论政治完全没有意义，而且很累。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>penance</b></td><td>/ˈpenəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>忏悔</td><td>do penance for sins<br>为罪过做忏悔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the whole winter doing penance for what he had done.  他整个冬天都在为自己的所作所为忏悔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>peruse</b></td><td>/pəˈruːz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>细读</td><td>peruse the contract carefully<br>仔细通读合同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She perused the contract carefully before signing anything at all.  她在签任何东西之前都仔细通读了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 examine</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæmɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>细查，仔细审视</td><td>examine the evidence closely<br>仔细审查证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scientist examined every slide under the microscope twice.  这位科学家把每张切片在显微镜下看了两遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scrutinize</td><td>/ˈskruːtənaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>仔细审查；细看</td><td>scrutinize the document<br>仔细审查文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Auditors will scrutinize every receipt before approving the annual budget report.  审计员会在批准年度预算报告前仔细审查每一张收据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glance</td><td>/ˈɡlæns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>扫视，一瞥</td><td>glance at his watch<br>看一眼手表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She glanced at her watch and hurried toward the front door.  她看了一眼手表，匆匆走向前门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glimpse</td><td>/ˈɡlɪmps/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>一瞥，瞥见</td><td>catch a glimpse of her<br>瞥见她一眼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I caught only a glimpse of the singer through the crowd.  我只在人群中瞥见了歌手一眼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scan</td><td>/ˈskæn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>细看；浏览，扫视</td><td>scan the horizon<br>扫视地平线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He scanned the horizon for any sign of the missing boat.  他扫视地平线，寻找失踪小船的踪影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 skim</td><td>/ˈskɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>略读，浏览</td><td>skim the report quickly<br>快速浏览报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She skimmed the long report during the short taxi ride.  她在短短的出租车路程中浏览了那份长报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pervade</b></td><td>/pərˈveɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>弥漫，充满</td><td>pervade the whole house<br>弥漫整座房子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A faint smell of lavender pervaded the whole house that summer.  那个夏天，淡淡的薰衣草香弥漫了整座房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 interpenetrate</del></td><td>/ˌɪntərˈpɛnətreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>相互渗透，贯穿</td><td>interpenetrate two cultures<br>使两种文化相互渗透</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Folk melodies and classical forms interpenetrate throughout his early symphonies.  民间旋律与古典形式在他早期交响曲中相互渗透。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 percolate</td><td>/ˈpɜːrkəˌleɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>渗透，慢慢扩散</td><td>percolate through the soil<br>渗透过土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rainwater slowly percolated through the sandy soil into the well.  雨水缓慢渗过沙质土壤流入井中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 riddle</td><td>/ˈrɪdəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>谜语，难题</td><td>answer a riddle<br>解答谜语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man asked a riddle that nobody in the village could answer.  老人问了一个村里没人能答的谜语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 suffuse</del></td><td>/səˈfjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（色彩等）弥漫，染遍，充满</td><td>suffused with warm light<br>沐浴在暖光中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm evening light suffused the quiet room with a golden glow.  傍晚的暖光把安静的房间染成金色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transfuse</td><td>/trænsˈfjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>注入，灌输；输血</td><td>transfuse new energy<br>注入新的活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young coach transfused fresh confidence into the struggling team.  这位年轻教练把新的信心注入陷入困境的球队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pessimistic</b></td><td>/ˌpesɪˈmɪstɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲观的</td><td>a pessimistic outlook<br>悲观的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained pessimistic about the company's chances of survival.  他对公司生存的机会仍然持悲观态度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bearish</td><td>/ˈberɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>看跌的，悲观的</td><td>a bearish outlook<br>看跌的前景预期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Analysts remained bearish about the housing market all year.  分析师全年对房地产市场都持看跌态度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 defeatist</td><td>/dɪˈfiːtɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>失败主义的，认输的</td><td>a defeatist attitude<br>失败主义的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her defeatist attitude discouraged the rest of the team.  她认输的态度让队里其他人也丧了气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despairing</td><td>/dɪˈsperɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>绝望的，失去希望的</td><td>a despairing tone<br>绝望的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spoke in a despairing tone about the coming harvest.  他用绝望的语气谈起即将到来的收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 downbeat</td><td>/ˈdaʊnˌbiːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲观的，低沉的</td><td>a downbeat forecast<br>悲观的预测</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister gave a surprisingly downbeat assessment of the economy.  这位部长对经济给出了出人意料的悲观评估。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hopeless</td><td>/ˈhoʊpləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无望的，绝望的</td><td>a hopeless situation<br>毫无希望的局面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctors described his condition as sadly hopeless.  医生们说他的病情令人遗憾地毫无希望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 optimistic</td><td>/ˌɑːptəˈmɪstɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>乐观的</td><td>remain cautiously optimistic<br>保持谨慎乐观</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite the losses, the founders remained optimistic about next year.  尽管亏损，创始人们对明年依然乐观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>petition</b></td><td>/pəˈtɪʃn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>请愿书；请求</td><td>sign a petition<br>在请愿书上签名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands signed the petition to save the old public library.  数千人签署请愿书，要求保住这座旧的公共图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appeal</td><td>/əˈpiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>上诉；恳求，呼吁</td><td>file an appeal<br>提出上诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lawyers will file an appeal against the verdict next week.  她的律师下周将针对判决提出上诉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cry</td><td>/ˈkraɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>呼喊，恳求；哭</td><td>a cry for help<br>求助的呼声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers sent out a desperate cry for outside help.  村民们发出绝望的呼声请求外界援助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entreaty</td><td>/enˈtriːti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恳求，乞求</td><td>ignore her entreaties<br>无视她的恳求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She ignored his desperate entreaties and closed the door firmly.  她无视他绝望的恳求，坚决关上了门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pleading</td><td>/ˈpliːdɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>恳求，请求；恳求的</td><td>a pleading tone<br>恳求的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked at her with pleading eyes but said nothing.  他用恳求的眼神看着她，却什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 solicitation</td><td>/səˌlɪsɪˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恳求，请求，募集</td><td>solicitation of donations<br>募捐请求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity's solicitation of donations raised money for flood victims.  这家慈善机构的募捐为洪灾受害者筹得资金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supplication</td><td>/ˌsʌpləˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恳求，祈求</td><td>kneel in supplication<br>跪地祈求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilgrims knelt in silent supplication before the ancient shrine.  朝圣者在古老神殿前默默跪地祈求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 adjure</del></td><td>/əˈdʒʊr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>郑重恳求，恳请</td><td>adjure someone to speak<br>恳求某人开口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge adjured the witness to tell the whole truth.  法官恳请证人说出全部真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beseech</td><td>/bɪˈsiːtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，哀求</td><td>beseech him for mercy<br>恳求他宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoners beseeched the governor for mercy in a long letter.  囚犯们在一封长信中恳求州长开恩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conjure</td><td>/kənˈdʒʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，央求；使浮现于脑海</td><td>conjure him to stay<br>恳求他留下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She conjured him to stay until the doctor finally arrived.  她恳求他留下，直到医生终于赶来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entreat</td><td>/ɪnˈtriːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求</td><td>entreat her to return<br>恳求她回来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She entreated him to return before their mother learned of the quarrel.  她恳求他在母亲得知这场争吵之前回来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 impetrate</del></td><td>/ˈɪmpɪtreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，祈求得到</td><td>impetrate divine mercy<br>祈求神的宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers gathered to impetrate rain from the heavens.  村民聚集起来向上天祈求降雨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 implore</td><td>/ˌɪmˈplɔːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，哀求</td><td>implore for help<br>苦苦求助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She implored the judge to give her brother another chance.  她恳求法官再给她弟弟一次机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 importune</del></td><td>/ˌɪmpɔːrˈtuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，迫切请求</td><td>importune him for money<br>向他苦苦求钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Beggars importuned travelers for coins outside the station all evening.  乞丐整晚在车站外向旅客苦苦讨要零钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plead</td><td>/ˈpliːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>恳求；辩护，申辩</td><td>plead for mercy<br>恳求宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner pleaded for mercy in a trembling, exhausted voice.  囚犯用颤抖疲惫的声音恳求宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 solicit</td><td>/səˈlɪsɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>请求，征求；招揽</td><td>solicit donations<br>募集捐款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity solicited funds from local businesses all winter.  该慈善机构整个冬天向本地企业募捐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supplicate</td><td>/ˈsʌplɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>恳求，乞求</td><td>supplicate for forgiveness<br>乞求宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner knelt to supplicate for forgiveness before the military tribunal.  囚犯跪下，在军事法庭前乞求宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>petrify</b></td><td>/ˈpetrɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使僵化，使失去活力</td><td>petrify a living tradition<br>使活的传统僵化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Endless rules can petrify a living tradition into empty ritual.  无穷的规矩会使活的传统僵化为空洞的仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 damp</td><td>/ˈdæmp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>抑制，使沮丧；潮湿的</td><td>damp their enthusiasm<br>打消他们的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain did not damp our enthusiasm for the trip.  下雨并没有打消我们对这次旅行的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dampen</td><td>/ˈdæmpən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，压抑（感情、精力等），泼冷水</td><td>dampen his enthusiasm<br>打击他的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Losing the first game did nothing to dampen the team's spirits.  首场失利丝毫没有打击球队的士气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deaden</td><td>/ˈdedən/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使减弱，使麻木</td><td>deaden the pain<br>减轻疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick carpets deaden the sound of footsteps upstairs.  厚地毯减弱了楼上的脚步声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 devitalize</del></td><td>/diːˈvaɪtəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使失去活力</td><td>devitalize the local economy<br>使当地经济失去活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of neglect devitalized the once thriving downtown district.  多年的忽视使曾经繁荣的市中心失去活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enervate</del></td><td>/ˈenərveɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使衰弱</td><td>enervate the whole team<br>使全队疲弱无力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long heat wave enervated the whole team before the final match.  漫长的热浪在决赛前使全队疲弱无力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 lobotomize</del></td><td>/ləˈbɑtəmaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>切除脑白质；使麻木呆滞</td><td>lobotomize the audience<br>使观众变得麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hours of mindless television seemed to lobotomize the whole family.  几小时无脑电视似乎让全家人都变得麻木呆滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ossify</del></td><td>/ˈɑːsɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）硬化，（使）僵化</td><td>ossify into dogma<br>僵化为教条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Over the decades the bold vision began to ossify into rigid dogma.  几十年间，那个大胆的愿景开始僵化为死板的教条。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brace</td><td>/ˈbreɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使振作，鼓励；支撑</td><td>brace yourself for impact<br>做好承受冲击准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cup of hot tea braced him for the long night.  一杯热茶让他为漫长的夜晚振作起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 energize</td><td>/ˈenərdʒaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使充满活力，激励</td><td>energize the whole team<br>使整个团队振奋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new coach energized a team that had lost hope.  新教练让失去希望的球队重新振奋起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enliven</td><td>/ɪnˈlaɪvn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使活跃，使生动</td><td>enliven a dull party<br>让沉闷的聚会活跃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her jokes enlivened an otherwise dull afternoon meeting.  她的玩笑让本来沉闷的下午会议活跃起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 invigorate</td><td>/ɪnˈvɪɡəreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使精神，使强壮，鼓舞激励</td><td>invigorate the local economy<br>振兴当地经济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk in cold air always invigorates my tired mind.  在冷空气中短暂散步总能让我疲惫的头脑重新振作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quicken</td><td>/ˈkwɪkən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>加快；使有生气，激活</td><td>quicken the pace<br>加快步伐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The good news quickened her pulse and lifted her spirits.  好消息让她心跳加快，精神振奋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stimulate</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激发；促进</td><td>stimulate economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm rain stimulated the seeds to sprout within a week.  温暖的雨水刺激种子一周内发芽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vitalize</td><td>/ˈvaɪtəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赋予活力，激励</td><td>vitalize the local economy<br>使当地经济充满活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New investment vitalized the sleepy town within just two years.  新投资在两年内使这个沉寂的小镇焕发活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 vivify</del></td><td>/ˈvɪvɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使生动，赋予生命</td><td>vivify a dull narrative<br>使乏味的叙述生动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vivid dialogue vivifies an otherwise dull historical narrative.  生动的对话使原本乏味的历史叙述活了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>petty</b></td><td>/ˈpeti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>petty complaints<br>琐碎的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted the meeting on petty complaints about parking spaces.  他把会议浪费在关于停车位的琐碎抱怨上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsiderable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>an inconsiderable sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk is small but by no means inconsiderable.  风险虽小，却绝非微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 measly</td><td>/ˈmiːzli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>少得可怜的，微不足道的</td><td>a measly ten dollars<br>少得可怜的十美元</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered a measly ten dollars for a painting worth several hundred.  他为一幅值几百美元的画只出了少得可怜的十美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minute</td><td>/maɪˈnjuːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的；细致的</td><td>a minute quantity<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even a minute trace of the chemical can spoil the batch.  即使极微量的这种化学品也会毁掉整批产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paltry</td><td>/ˈpɔːltri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的；微不足道的</td><td>a paltry sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a paltry sum for a painting worth thousands of dollars.  他们为一幅价值数千美元的画只出了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peanut</td><td>/ˈpiːnət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>微不足道的小钱；花生</td><td>work for peanuts<br>拿极少的钱干活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They paid him peanuts for a whole month of hard work.  他辛苦干了整整一个月，只拿到极少的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifling</td><td>/ˈtraɪflɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>a trifling sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the missing coins as a trifling sum not worth reporting.  他把丢失的硬币当作微不足道的小钱，不值得上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considerable</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（数量上）可观（而值得注意）的</td><td>a considerable sum<br>一笔可观的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The renovation required a considerable amount of money and nearly two years of work.  这次翻修耗费了可观的资金和近两年的工期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 important</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的</td><td>an important decision<br>一个重要的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sleep is as important as diet for a young athlete.  对年轻运动员来说，睡眠和饮食一样重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 material</td><td>/məˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>物质的；重要的；材料</td><td>material comforts<br>物质上的享受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cared little for material wealth and lived very simply.  他不太在意物质财富，生活极为简朴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>phenomenal</b></td><td>/fəˈnɑːmɪnl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，非凡的</td><td>phenomenal success<br>非凡的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel enjoyed phenomenal success in a dozen countries.  她的第一部小说在十几个国家取得了非凡的成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 especial</td><td>/əˈspeʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特别的，格外的</td><td>of especial importance<br>具有特别的重要性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This letter is of especial importance to the family history.  这封信对家族史有特别重要的意义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exceptional</td><td>/ɪkˈsepʃənl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>例外的，特别的，非凡的</td><td>an exceptional talent<br>非凡的天赋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She showed exceptional talent for mathematics at a very young age.  她在很小的年纪就表现出非凡的数学天赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extraordinary</td><td>/ɪkˈstrɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，特别的</td><td>extraordinary courage<br>非凡的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took extraordinary courage to report what she had seen.  举报她所见之事需要非凡的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peculiar</td><td>/pəˈkjuːliər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；特有的</td><td>a peculiar smell<br>一股怪味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a peculiar smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股奇怪的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preternatural</td><td>/ˌpriːtərˈnætʃrəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>超乎寻常的</td><td>preternatural calm<br>异乎寻常的平静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot showed preternatural calm while the engine was clearly failing.  发动机明显故障时，飞行员表现出超乎寻常的镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rare</td><td>/ˈrer/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀少的，罕见的</td><td>a rare bird<br>罕见的鸟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is rare to see snow here in April.  四月这里下雪很罕见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 singular</td><td>/ˈsɪŋɡjələr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>罕见的，非凡的；单数的</td><td>a singular achievement<br>非凡的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He showed singular courage during the long rescue.  他在漫长的营救中表现出非凡的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncommon</td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不常见的，罕有的</td><td>not uncommon<br>并不罕见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such mistakes are not uncommon among beginners.  这类错误在初学者中并不罕见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unusual</td><td>/ʌnˈjuːʒuəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，罕见的</td><td>an unusual coincidence<br>罕见的巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was an unusual coincidence that both letters arrived together.  两封信同时到达是个罕见的巧合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 common</td><td>/ˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，常见的；共有的</td><td>a common mistake<br>常见的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spelling errors are a common problem in student essays.  拼写错误是学生作文中常见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 normal</td><td>/ˈnɔːrməl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>正常的；常态</td><td>back to normal<br>恢复正常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everything returned to normal after the power was restored.  供电恢复后一切都回归正常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ordinary</td><td>/ˈɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普通的，平常的</td><td>just an ordinary weekday<br>只是平常的工作日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked like an ordinary commuter waiting for the morning bus.  他看上去就像个等早班公交的普通上班族。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 typical</td><td>/ˈtɪpəkəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>典型的，一贯的</td><td>a typical example<br>典型的例子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This building is a typical example of the local style.  这栋建筑是当地风格的典型代表。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>philanthropic</b></td><td>/ˌfɪlənˈθrɑːpɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>博爱的，为他人着想的</td><td>philanthropic donations<br>慈善捐赠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Philanthropic donations paid for the new hospital wing for sick children.  慈善捐赠支付了收治病童的医院新翼楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 altruistic</td><td>/ˌæltruˈɪstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无私的，利他的</td><td>altruistic motives<br>利他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her altruistic work in the clinic earned wide public respect.  她在诊所无私的工作赢得了公众广泛尊敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beneficent</td><td>/bɪˈnefɪsnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（对个人或社会）有益的</td><td>a beneficent influence<br>有益的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her long service had a beneficent influence on the whole village.  她多年的服务对整个村子产生了有益的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 benevolent</td><td>/bəˈnevələnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，行善的</td><td>a benevolent employer<br>仁慈的雇主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A benevolent stranger paid for the family's train tickets.  一位仁慈的陌生人为这家人买了火车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 eleemosynary</del></td><td>/ˌeləˈmɑːsəneri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慈善的，施舍的</td><td>an eleemosynary institution<br>慈善机构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hospital began as an eleemosynary institution for the poor.  这家医院最初是为穷人设立的慈善机构。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 good</td><td>/ˈɡʊd/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的，好的</td><td>a good opportunity<br>有利的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This is a good moment to renegotiate the contract.  这是重新谈判合同的有利时机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 humanitarian</td><td>/ˌhjuːmænəˈteriən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>人道主义的，博爱的</td><td>urgent humanitarian aid<br>紧急人道主义援助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several agencies sent humanitarian supplies to the flooded region.  几家机构向洪灾地区运送了人道主义物资。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 misanthropic</td><td>/ˌmɪsənˈθrɑpɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>厌恶人类的，愤世嫉俗的</td><td>a misanthropic recluse<br>厌世的隐居者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The misanthropic old writer refused every invitation he received.  这位厌世的老作家拒绝了他收到的每份邀请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>philistine</b></td><td>/ˈfɪlɪstiːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>市侩（注重物质而鄙视智慧或艺术的人）</td><td>a cultural philistine<br>文化上的市侩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed modern poetry as nonsense, revealing himself a complete philistine.  他把现代诗贬为胡言乱语，暴露出自己是个十足的市侩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lowbrow</td><td>/ˈloʊbraʊ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>低俗的；文化修养低的人</td><td>lowbrow entertainment<br>低俗的娱乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He openly enjoyed lowbrow comedies and felt no shame about it.  他公开喜爱低俗喜剧，对此毫不羞愧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 materialist</td><td>/məˈtɪriəlɪst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>物质主义者，实利主义者</td><td>a crass materialist<br>粗鄙的物质主义者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics dismissed the tycoon as a crass materialist without taste.  批评者把这位大亨说成毫无品味的粗鄙物质主义者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 highbrow</td><td>/ˈhaɪˌbraʊ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>高雅的；文化修养高的人</td><td>highbrow literature<br>高雅的文学作品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine publishes highbrow essays on art and philosophy.  这份杂志刊登关于艺术与哲学的高雅文章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>phlegmatic</b></td><td>/fleɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静的，无感情的，淡漠的</td><td>a phlegmatic temperament<br>冷静的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her phlegmatic response to the crisis calmed the entire staff.  她对危机的冷静反应让全体员工镇定下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 affectless</del></td><td>/ˈæfektləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无情感的，冷漠的</td><td>an affectless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness described the crime in a flat, affectless tone.  证人用平淡无情感的语调描述这桩罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apathetic</td><td>/ˌæpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠的，无兴趣的</td><td>apathetic voters<br>冷漠的选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most students seemed apathetic about the election and did not vote.  大多数学生对这次选举漠不关心，也没有投票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cold-blooded</td><td>/ˌkoʊldˈblʌdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷血的，无感情的</td><td>a cold-blooded killer<br>冷血的杀手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the crime as cold-blooded and carefully planned.  法庭形容这起罪行冷血且经过精心策划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emotionless</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无感情的，冷漠的</td><td>an emotionless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He read the verdict in a flat, emotionless tone.  他用平板毫无感情的语调宣读了判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impassible</td><td>/ɪmˈpæsəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无痛感的，不动感情的</td><td>an impassible expression<br>毫无表情的神色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard stood impassible while the crowd shouted insults.  人群叫骂时，卫兵毫无表情地站着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 numb</td><td>/nʌm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>麻木的，无感情的</td><td>numb with grief<br>因悲伤而麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Numb with grief, she answered the reporters' questions in a flat voice.  她因悲伤而麻木，用平板的声音回答记者的提问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stoic</td><td>/ˈstoʊɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐忍的，冷静的</td><td>stoic acceptance of pain<br>隐忍地承受痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her stoic acceptance of pain impressed even the veteran nurses.  她隐忍地承受痛苦，连资深护士都为之动容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stolid</td><td>/ˈstɑːlɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无动于衷的，感情麻木的</td><td>a stolid unsmiling guard<br>麻木不笑的卫兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A stolid unsmiling guard stood beside the gate all afternoon.  一名麻木不笑的卫兵整个下午守在门旁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 demonstrative</td><td>/dɪˈmɑːnstrətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感情外露的</td><td>a demonstrative greeting<br>热情外露的问候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her demonstrative hugs surprised colleagues used to formal handshakes.  她热情的拥抱让习惯正式握手的同事很惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 emotional</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感情丰富的；动情的</td><td>an emotional farewell speech<br>动情的告别演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became emotional when describing his father's last letter.  描述父亲最后一封信时他动了感情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fervent</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满感情的，热情洋溢的</td><td>a fervent believer<br>热切的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a fervent believer in free public libraries for everyone.  她热切地相信应该为所有人提供免费的公共图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fervid</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的；热情的</td><td>a fervid supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fervid speech moved even the quietest people in the room.  他热情洋溢的演讲连房间里最安静的人都被感动了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impassioned</td><td>/ɪmˈpæʃənd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨激昂的，充满激情的</td><td>an impassioned speech<br>慷慨激昂的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an impassioned defense of public libraries.  她慷慨激昂地为公共图书馆辩护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 passionate</td><td>/ˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，充满激情的</td><td>a passionate defense of art<br>对艺术的热情辩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is passionate about protecting the city's old neighborhoods.  她对保护城市的老街区充满热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vehement</td><td>/ˈviːəmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（情感）强烈的，热情的</td><td>vehement opposition<br>强烈的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a vehement protest against the new tax.  她对新税提出了强烈的抗议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>piddling</b></td><td>/ˈpɪdlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>a piddling sum of money<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a piddling sum for months of demanding work.  他们为数月繁重的工作只给了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsiderable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>an inconsiderable sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk is small but by no means inconsiderable.  风险虽小，却绝非微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 measly</td><td>/ˈmiːzli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>少得可怜的，微不足道的</td><td>a measly ten dollars<br>少得可怜的十美元</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered a measly ten dollars for a painting worth several hundred.  他为一幅值几百美元的画只出了少得可怜的十美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minute</td><td>/maɪˈnjuːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的；细致的</td><td>a minute quantity<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even a minute trace of the chemical can spoil the batch.  即使极微量的这种化学品也会毁掉整批产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paltry</td><td>/ˈpɔːltri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的；微不足道的</td><td>a paltry sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a paltry sum for a painting worth thousands of dollars.  他们为一幅价值数千美元的画只出了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peanut</td><td>/ˈpiːnət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>微不足道的小钱；花生</td><td>work for peanuts<br>拿极少的钱干活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They paid him peanuts for a whole month of hard work.  他辛苦干了整整一个月，只拿到极少的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifling</td><td>/ˈtraɪflɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>a trifling sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the missing coins as a trifling sum not worth reporting.  他把丢失的硬币当作微不足道的小钱，不值得上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considerable</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（数量上）可观（而值得注意）的</td><td>a considerable sum<br>一笔可观的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The renovation required a considerable amount of money and nearly two years of work.  这次翻修耗费了可观的资金和近两年的工期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 important</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的</td><td>an important decision<br>一个重要的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sleep is as important as diet for a young athlete.  对年轻运动员来说，睡眠和饮食一样重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 material</td><td>/məˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>物质的；重要的；材料</td><td>material comforts<br>物质上的享受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cared little for material wealth and lived very simply.  他不太在意物质财富，生活极为简朴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>piebald</b></td><td>/ˈpaɪbɔːld/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有黑白斑块的</td><td>a piebald horse<br>黑白花的马</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A piebald horse grazed quietly beside the wooden fence.  一匹黑白花的马在木栅栏旁静静吃草。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assorted</td><td>/əˈsɔːrtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>各式各样的，混合的</td><td>assorted chocolates<br>什锦巧克力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She brought a box of assorted chocolates to the office.  她带了一盒什锦巧克力到办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eclectic</td><td>/ɪˈklektɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>博采众长的；不拘一格的</td><td>an eclectic collection<br>混合多元的收藏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her taste in music is remarkably eclectic and hard to label.  她的音乐品味相当博杂，难以归类。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heterogeneous</td><td>/ˌhetərəˈdʒiːniəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>异质的，混杂的</td><td>a heterogeneous mixture<br>混杂的混合物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The class is a heterogeneous mixture of ages and backgrounds.  这个班是年龄和背景的混杂组合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hybrid</td><td>/ˈhaɪbrɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>杂交品种，混合品种</td><td>a hardy hybrid<br>耐寒的杂交种</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rose is a hybrid bred for cold northern gardens.  这种玫瑰是为寒冷北方花园培育的杂交品种。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magpie</td><td>/ˈmæɡˌpaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喜鹊；爱收集杂物者</td><td>a magpie collection<br>杂七杂八的收藏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His study is a magpie hoard of maps, coins and shells.  他的书房是地图、硬币和贝壳的杂堆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mixed</td><td>/ˈmɪkst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混合的，掺杂的；好坏交织的</td><td>mixed feelings<br>复杂的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I have mixed feelings about moving abroad.  对于出国我心情复杂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 motley</td><td>/ˈmɑːtli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混杂的，富于变化的</td><td>a motley crowd<br>混杂的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A motley crowd of farmers, students, and shopkeepers filled the town square.  农民、学生和店主组成的混杂人群挤满了市镇广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 promiscuous</td><td>/prəˈmɪskjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>混杂的，不加区别的</td><td>a promiscuous mixture<br>杂乱的混合物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shop sold a promiscuous mixture of antiques and cheap junk.  这家店卖的是古董和廉价杂物的混杂堆积。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 varied</td><td>/ˈverid/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>各式各样的，多变的</td><td>a varied diet<br>多样的饮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A varied diet keeps both cook and eater interested.  多样的饮食让做饭的和吃饭的都有兴致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 homogeneous</td><td>/ˌhoʊməˈdʒiːniəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>同质的，同类的</td><td>a homogeneous population<br>同质的人口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The town's population is unusually homogeneous in language and faith.  该镇人口在语言和信仰上异常同质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pigment</b></td><td>/ˈpɪɡmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>颜料</td><td>natural plant pigment<br>天然植物颜料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ancient painters ground minerals to make pigment for their wall paintings.  古代画家把矿石磨碎，为壁画制作颜料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 colorant</td><td>/ˈkʌlərənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>着色剂，色素</td><td>food colorant<br>食品着色剂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company replaced artificial colorant with extracts from beets.  该公司用甜菜提取物替代了人工着色剂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dye</td><td>/ˈdaɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>染料；染色</td><td>natural indigo dye<br>天然靛蓝染料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The weavers still use natural indigo dye for their cloth.  织工们仍用天然靛蓝染料给布料染色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stain</td><td>/ˈsteɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>沾污，玷污；污点</td><td>stain his reputation<br>玷污他的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal stained the family name for two whole generations.  这桩丑闻让这个家族的名声蒙污两代人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paint</td><td>/ˈpeɪnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>涂料，颜料；绘画</td><td>a coat of paint<br>一层涂料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fresh coat of paint transformed the shabby old door.  一层新涂料让破旧的老门焕然一新。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tincture</td><td>/ˈtɪŋktʃər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>淡色，色彩；酊剂</td><td>a tincture of red<br>一抹淡淡的红色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A faint tincture of red appeared along the evening clouds.  傍晚的云边泛起一抹淡淡的红色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tinge</td><td>/tɪndʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给…着上少量的色彩</td><td>tinge the sky pink<br>把天空染成淡粉色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The setting sun tinged the sky a delicate shade of pink.  落日把天空染成淡淡的粉色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tint</td><td>/ˈtɪnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>淡色调；给…着色</td><td>a greenish tint<br>淡淡的绿色调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old photograph had acquired a faint yellow tint.  那张老照片已泛上淡淡的黄色调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 blanch</td><td>/ˈblæntʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使变白，漂白；发白</td><td>blanch the almonds<br>把杏仁焯白</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He blanched visibly when the bill was handed over.  账单递来时他明显脸色发白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pilfer</b></td><td>/ˈpɪlfər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>偷窃</td><td>pilfer office supplies<br>偷拿办公用品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone had been pilfering office supplies from the storage room for months.  几个月来一直有人从储藏室偷拿办公用品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appropriate</td><td>/əˈproʊprieɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>私自挪用；适当的</td><td>appropriate public funds<br>挪用公款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials were accused of appropriating public money for private use.  官员们被指控将公款挪为私用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 filch</td><td>/ˈfɪltʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>偷取，小偷小摸</td><td>filch a few coins<br>偷走几枚硬币</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy filched a few coins from his father's coat pocket.  男孩从他父亲的外衣口袋里偷了几枚硬币。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hook</td><td>/ˈhʊk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>钩住，连接；钩子</td><td>hook the trailer on<br>把拖车挂上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hooked the two cables together before starting the engine.  她把两根电缆连接好才启动发动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lift</td><td>/ˈlɪft/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>偷窃；举起，提高</td><td>lift a wallet<br>偷走钱包</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A pickpocket lifted his wallet on the crowded subway platform.  一个扒手在拥挤的地铁站台上偷走了他的钱包。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pinch</td><td>/ˈpɪntʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>短缺，窘迫；捏</td><td>feel the pinch<br>感到经济窘迫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small shops began to feel the pinch as rents rose sharply.  租金猛涨后小店铺开始感到经济窘迫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 purloin</del></td><td>/pɜːrˈlɔɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>偷窃</td><td>purloin a secret document<br>偷走一份密件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clerk managed to purloin a secret document from the locked cabinet.  那名职员设法从上锁的柜子里偷走了一份密件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 steal</td><td>/ˈstiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>潜行，悄悄移动；偷</td><td>steal into the room<br>悄悄溜进房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Each night he would steal quietly out of the house.  他每晚都会悄悄溜出房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thieve</td><td>/ˈθiːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>偷窃，行窃</td><td>thieve from neighbors<br>偷邻居的东西</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two brothers thieved from local shops for several months.  两兄弟连续几个月偷本地商店的东西。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>precocious</b></td><td>/prɪˈkoʊʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>早熟的</td><td>a precocious child<br>早熟的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The precocious girl was reading adult novels before she turned eight.  那个早熟的女孩不到八岁就在读成人小说了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 early</td><td>/ˈɜːrli/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>早的，提前的</td><td>an early talent<br>早年显露的才华</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her early talent for languages surprised all of her teachers.  她早年显露的语言才华让所有老师惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 premature</td><td>/ˌpriːməˈtʃʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过早的，早熟的</td><td>a premature conclusion<br>过早下的结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drawing a conclusion now would be premature and possibly misleading.  现在下结论过早，还可能造成误导。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unseasonable</td><td>/ʌnˈsizənəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合季节的；不合时宜的</td><td>unseasonable warmth in January<br>一月里反常的暖和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unseasonable heat in March confused the valley's fruit trees.  三月反常的高温让谷中的果树错乱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pillory</b></td><td>/ˈpɪləri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>当众嘲弄</td><td>pillory him in public<br>当众嘲弄他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The newspapers pilloried the minister for weeks after his careless remark.  他说了那句轻率的话后，报纸连续几周对这位大臣公开嘲弄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abase</td><td>/əˈbeɪs/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>降低（地位、威望、尊严）</td><td>abase oneself before someone<br>在某人面前自贬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He would not abase himself by begging for the job.  他不愿低声下气地乞求这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debase</td><td>/dɪˈbeɪs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>贬低，贬损</td><td>debase the currency<br>使货币贬值</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Politicians who lie to win votes debase the office they hold.  为拉票而撒谎的政客贬损了他们所担任的职务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degrade</td><td>/dɪˈɡreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>贬低，使降级；使退化</td><td>degrade the quality<br>降低质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such insulting jokes degrade everyone who repeats them.  这类侮辱性的笑话贬低了每个转述它们的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 denounce</td><td>/dɪˈnaʊns/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开指责</td><td>denounce corruption<br>公开指责腐败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local leaders publicly denounced the corruption exposed by the newspaper.  当地领导人公开谴责报纸曝光的腐败行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 humiliate</td><td>/hjuːˈmɪlieɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>羞辱，使丧失尊严</td><td>humiliate him in public<br>当众羞辱他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher never humiliated a student for asking a simple question.  这位老师从不因学生问简单的问题而羞辱他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ridicule</td><td>/ˈrɪdɪkjuːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑；讥讽；愚弄</td><td>an object of ridicule<br>被嘲笑的对象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Classmates began to ridicule him for his old shoes and worn coat.  同学们开始因他的旧鞋和破外套而嘲笑他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 carol</td><td>/ˈkærəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>欢快颂唱，歌颂；颂歌</td><td>carol his praises<br>歌颂他的功绩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children caroled cheerfully outside every door on the street.  孩子们在街上每户门前欢快地颂唱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogize</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>称赞；颂扬</td><td>eulogize the late leader<br>颂扬已故领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues gathered to eulogize the scientist who had trained them all.  同事们聚在一起颂扬这位培养了他们所有人的科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exalt</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬；提升（地位）</td><td>exalt to the skies<br>推崇到极点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics exalt her early novels as masterpieces of the era.  评论家将她早期的小说赞为那个时代的杰作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pilot</b></td><td>/ˈpaɪlət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>试验性的，初步的；试行</td><td>a pilot program<br>试点项目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school will pilot the new reading program in three classes.  学校将在三个班级试行这个新的阅读项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 developmental</td><td>/dɪˌveləpˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>发展的，开发阶段的</td><td>the developmental stage<br>开发试验阶段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The engine is still at a developmental stage and untested.  这台发动机仍处于开发阶段，尚未测试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 experimental</td><td>/ɪkˌsperɪˈmentəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>实验的，试验性的</td><td>experimental data<br>实验数据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The drug remains experimental and is not yet approved for sale.  该药仍处试验阶段，尚未获批销售。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 model</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>典范，模范；模型</td><td>a model of efficiency<br>效率的典范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clinic became a model of efficiency for the region.  这家诊所成了全区效率的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 test</td><td>/ˈtest/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>试验，测试</td><td>a test flight<br>一次试飞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers scheduled a test flight for early next month.  工程师们把试飞安排在下月初。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trial</td><td>/ˈtraɪəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>试验；审判</td><td>a clinical trial<br>一项临床试验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The drug performed well in a small clinical trial.  该药在一项小型临床试验中表现良好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conduct</td><td>/kənˈdʌkt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>引导，带领；实施</td><td>conduct visitors around<br>带领参观者四处走</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A young monk conducted the visitors around the ancient temple.  一位年轻僧人带领参观者游览这座古寺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 direct</td><td>/dəˈrekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直接的，径直的</td><td>a direct flight<br>直飞航班</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ask for a direct answer instead of a long explanation.  要个直接的答复，而不是长篇解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guide</td><td>/ˈɡaɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>引导，带路</td><td>guide the ship safely<br>安全引领船只</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A local pilot guided the ship safely through the narrow channel.  一名当地领航员将船安全引过狭窄的航道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lead</td><td>/liːd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引导，带领</td><td>lead a team of interns<br>带领一队实习生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He leads new employees through every step of the process.  他带着新员工走完流程的每一步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 route</td><td>/ˈruːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>路线；按路线发送</td><td>route the traffic around<br>把车流绕道引开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police routed the traffic around the flooded intersection.  警察把车流绕开被水淹的路口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 show</td><td>/ˈʃoʊ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>显示，展现；展览</td><td>show your feelings<br>表露感情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rarely shows his feelings, even to close friends.  他很少表露感情，连对好友也如此。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 steer</td><td>/ˈstɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>驾驶，掌舵；引导</td><td>steer the boat ashore<br>把船驶向岸边</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He steered the small boat carefully between the rocks.  他小心地把小船驶过礁石之间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eventual</td><td>/əˈventʃuəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>最终的，结果的</td><td>the eventual outcome<br>最终的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody could predict the eventual outcome of the long negotiation.  没人能预料这场漫长谈判的最终结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 final</td><td>/ˈfaɪnəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>最后的，最终的</td><td>the final decision<br>最终决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final chapter explains why the theory was abandoned.  最后一章解释了该理论为何被放弃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ultimate</td><td>/ˈʌltəmət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>最终的；根本的</td><td>the ultimate goal<br>最终目标</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Independence was the ultimate aim of their long struggle.  独立是他们长期斗争的最终目标。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 follow</td><td>/ˈfɑːloʊ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遵循，跟随</td><td>follow the rules<br>遵守规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 If you follow the instructions exactly, nothing can go wrong.  只要严格按说明操作，就不会出错。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pine</b></td><td>/paɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>渴望，思念</td><td>pine for home<br>思念家乡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After a year abroad he began to pine for the food of home.  在国外一年后，他开始渴望家乡的食物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ache</td><td>/ˈeɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>渴望；疼痛</td><td>ache for home<br>渴望回家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After three years abroad she ached for her hometown.  在国外三年后，她十分渴望回到故乡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crave</td><td>/kreɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>热望</td><td>crave attention<br>渴望关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After weeks in the desert, they craved nothing but cold clean water.  在沙漠里待了几周后，他们只渴望干净的凉水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dream</td><td>/ˈdriːm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>梦想，幻想；做梦</td><td>dream of fame<br>幻想成名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a child he dreamed of sailing around the world.  他小时候幻想过环球航行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hunger</td><td>/ˈhʌŋɡər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>渴望；饥饿</td><td>a hunger for knowledge<br>对知识的渴望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His hunger for knowledge kept him reading until dawn.  对知识的渴望让他读书到黎明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 itch</td><td>/ˈɪtʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>渴望；发痒</td><td>itch to travel<br>渴望去旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were itching to open their presents early.  孩子们急着想早点拆礼物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 long</td><td>/ˈlɔːŋ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>不及物动词、形容词</td><td>渴望；长的</td><td>long for company<br>渴望有人陪伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After months at sea he longed for his family's cooking.  在海上数月后，他渴望家人做的饭菜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lust</td><td>/ˈlʌst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>强烈欲望，渴望</td><td>a lust for power<br>对权力的强烈欲望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lust for power destroyed several old and valuable friendships.  他对权力的强烈欲望毁掉了几段珍贵的旧友情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 repine</del></td><td>/rɪˈpaɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>抱怨，表达不满；渴望</td><td>repine at one's fate<br>抱怨自己的命运</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rather than repine at his losses, he quietly started the business again.  他没有抱怨自己的损失，而是默默重新开始了生意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thirst</td><td>/ˈθɜːrst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>渴望；口渴</td><td>a thirst for knowledge<br>求知欲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He thirsted for recognition far more than for money.  他对认可的渴望远超对金钱的渴望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yearn</td><td>/ˈjɜːrn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>渴望，向往</td><td>yearn for freedom<br>渴望自由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years abroad he yearned for the food of his childhood.  在国外多年后，他渴望儿时的食物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abhor</td><td>/əbˈhɔːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>极度厌恶，深恶痛绝</td><td>abhor violence<br>痛恨暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She abhors any form of cruelty to animals.  她极度厌恶任何形式的虐待动物行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detest</td><td>/dɪˈtest/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>厌恶，憎恨</td><td>detest hypocrisy<br>厌恶虚伪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I detest waiting in long lines on hot days.  我讨厌在炎热的日子里排长队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loathe</td><td>/loʊð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>厌恶，讨厌</td><td>loathe the sight of<br>一见就厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He loathes the smell of boiled cabbage.  他讨厌煮卷心菜的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pinnacle</b></td><td>/ˈpɪnəkl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶峰，巅峰</td><td>the pinnacle of success<br>成功的顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At thirty she had reached the pinnacle of her profession.  三十岁时她已达到职业生涯的巅峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acme</td><td>/ˈækmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，极点</td><td>the acme of perfection<br>完美的极致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That performance marked the acme of her long career.  那场演出标志着她漫长职业生涯的顶峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apex</td><td>/ˈeɪˌpeks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，最高点</td><td>the apex of his career<br>他事业的顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At the apex of her fame she suddenly left the stage.  在名声的顶峰，她突然离开了舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 climax</td><td>/ˈklaɪˌmæks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>高潮，顶点</td><td>the climax of the story<br>故事的高潮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film builds slowly toward a violent and unexpected climax.  影片缓慢推向一个暴烈而意外的高潮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 culmination</td><td>/ˌkʌlməˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，高潮，最终结果</td><td>the culmination of efforts<br>努力的最终成果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prize was the culmination of twenty years of research.  这个奖项是二十年研究的最高成果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meridian</td><td>/məˈrɪdiən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，全盛期；子午线</td><td>at the meridian of his career<br>在他事业顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The composer died at the meridian of his creative powers.  这位作曲家在创作力顶峰时去世。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peak</td><td>/ˈpiːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>顶峰，最高点</td><td>at the peak of<br>在…的顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales peaked in December and then fell sharply in January.  销售在十二月达到顶峰，一月急剧下滑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 summit</td><td>/ˈsʌmɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点</td><td>reach the summit<br>登上顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The climbers reached the summit just before the storm closed in.  登山者在风暴逼近前登上顶峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 top</td><td>/ˈtɑːp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，最高处</td><td>at the top of the list<br>在名单最上方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her name sat at the top of the shortlist.  她的名字排在候选名单最上方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 zenith</td><td>/ˈzenɪθ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>最高点，巅峰</td><td>at the zenith of<br>在…的巅峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empire stood at the zenith of its power around that year.  大约在那一年，这个帝国正处于国力的巅峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bottom</td><td>/ˈbɑːtəm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>底部，最低点</td><td>at the bottom<br>在底部</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their team finished at the bottom of the league again.  他们的球队又一次排在联赛末位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nadir</td><td>/ˈneɪdɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>最低点，谷底</td><td>reach the nadir<br>到达最低点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His confidence reached its nadir after the third rejection.  第三次被拒后他的信心降到了最低点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pinpoint</b></td><td>/ˈpɪnpɔɪnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>非常精确的；精确定位或确认</td><td>pinpoint the exact cause<br>精确找出确切原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers finally pinpointed the exact cause of the power failure.  工程师们最终精确找出了停电的确切原因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accurate</td><td>/ˈækjərət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精确的，准确的</td><td>an accurate measurement<br>精确的测量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need accurate figures before making the final decision.  在做最终决定前我们需要准确的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 close</td><td>/kloʊz/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>关闭；结束</td><td>close the meeting<br>结束会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They closed the discussion before anyone could object.  他们在有人反对之前结束了讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delicate</td><td>/ˈdeləkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，精细的；脆弱的</td><td>a delicate distinction<br>微妙的区别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a delicate ear for the subtle differences between dialects.  她对方言之间的细微差别有很灵敏的耳朵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exact</td><td>/ɪɡˈzækt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>强索，征收；精确的</td><td>exact a heavy tax<br>征收重税</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The conquerors exacted tribute from every village in the valley.  征服者向谷中每个村庄强征贡赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fine</td><td>/ˈfaɪn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，精细的</td><td>a fine distinction<br>细微的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He drew a fine distinction between courage and recklessness.  他在勇敢与莽撞之间划出了细微的区别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hairline</td><td>/ˈherˌlaɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>极细的；发际线</td><td>a hairline crack<br>一道极细的裂缝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors found a hairline crack in the concrete support.  检查人员在混凝土支柱上发现一道极细的裂缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mathematical</td><td>/ˌmæθəˈmætɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>数学的；精确无误的</td><td>mathematical precision<br>数学般的精确</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancers moved with almost mathematical precision across the stage.  舞者们以近乎数学般的精确穿过舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 precise</td><td>/prɪˈsaɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精确的，明确的</td><td>precise measurements<br>精确的测量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need precise figures before we can approve the budget.  批准预算前我们需要精确的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refined</td><td>/rəˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精炼的，纯净的；有教养的</td><td>refined sugar<br>精制糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refined sugar has been stripped of every impurity.  精制糖已去除了所有杂质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rigorous</td><td>/ˈrɪɡərəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（天气）严酷的；严格的</td><td>a rigorous climate<br>严酷的气候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few crops survive the rigorous winters of the plateau.  很少有作物能度过高原严酷的冬天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 determine</td><td>/dəˈtɜːrmən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>判定，确定；决心</td><td>determine the cause<br>判定原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scientists could not determine the cause of the sudden outbreak.  科学家无法判定这次突发疫情的原因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguish</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>区分，辨别；使显著</td><td>distinguish right from wrong<br>分辨是非</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young children cannot always distinguish fantasy from reality.  幼儿并不总能区分幻想与现实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 locate</td><td>/ˈloʊˌkeɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>找出位置，定位</td><td>locate the leak<br>找出漏点位置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers finally located the leak beneath the kitchen floor.  工程师最终在厨房地板下找到了漏水处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 identify</td><td>/aɪˈdentəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>确认，辨认出</td><td>identify the main cause<br>确认主要原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers identified the main cause of the outbreak within days.  研究人员在几天内确认了疫情的主要原因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recognize</td><td>/ˈrekəɡˌnaɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>认出，辨识</td><td>recognize a familiar face<br>认出熟悉的面孔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He recognized her familiar handwriting on the faded old envelope.  他在褪色的旧信封上认出她熟悉的字迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spot</td><td>/ˈspɑːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>污点，斑点；地点</td><td>a spot on the shirt<br>衬衫上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A grease spot on the sleeve ruined his only white shirt.  袖子上的油污弄坏了他唯一的白衬衫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 coarse</td><td>/kɔːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，表面不平整的；粗俗的</td><td>coarse language<br>粗俗的言语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers used coarse language that shocked the visiting students.  工人们说的粗话让来访的学生们大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cursory</td><td>/ˈkɜːrsəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，不注意细节的</td><td>a cursory glance<br>匆匆一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cursory inspection missed the cracks running along the bridge supports.  一次匆忙的检查漏掉了桥墩上的裂缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rough</td><td>/ˈrʌf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，粗暴的；艰苦的</td><td>a rough surface<br>粗糙的表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road grew rough as we climbed into the hills.  我们爬上山时路变得坎坷不平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 estimate</td><td>/ˈestəmət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>估计，估算</td><td>a rough estimate<br>粗略的估算</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave only a rough estimate of the repair costs.  她只给出了修理费用的粗略估算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>piquant</b></td><td>/ˈpiːkənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人振奋的</td><td>a piquant remark<br>令人振奋的妙语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His piquant remarks kept the whole table laughing through dinner.  他令人振奋的妙语让整桌人整顿晚餐都在笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exciting</td><td>/ɪkˈsaɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人兴奋的，刺激的</td><td>an exciting flavor<br>令人兴奋的味道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chef added chili to give the sauce an exciting edge.  厨师加了辣椒，让酱汁有了刺激的味觉冲击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pungent</td><td>/ˈpʌndʒənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言辞）辛辣尖刻的；刺鼻的</td><td>pungent criticism<br>辛辣的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pungent criticism of the report left everyone at the table silent.  他对这份报告辛辣的批评让全桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 racy</td><td>/ˈreɪsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼生动的</td><td>a racy narrative style<br>生动的叙述风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her racy narrative style keeps readers turning pages late into the night.  她生动活泼的叙述风格让读者深夜还在翻页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stimulating</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>激励的，令人振奋的</td><td>a stimulating discussion<br>一场激发思考的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The seminar was the most stimulating class of the term.  这场研讨会是本学期最能激发思考的课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flat</td><td>/ˈflæt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调无光的，乏味的</td><td>a flat performance<br>平淡无味的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paint dried to a flat, lifeless gray.  油漆干成了一种无光而死板的灰色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pallid</td><td>/ˈpælɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无生气的，缺乏活力的</td><td>a pallid imitation<br>毫无生气的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The remake was a pallid imitation of a far livelier original.  这部翻拍片是对一部活力十足的原作的毫无生气的模仿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pique</b></td><td>/piːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起（兴趣）；使生气</td><td>pique her curiosity<br>激起她的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange title piqued her curiosity, so she borrowed the book.  奇怪的书名激起了她的好奇心，于是她借走了那本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arouse</td><td>/əˈraʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>唤起，激起；唤醒</td><td>arouse public suspicion<br>引起公众怀疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The missing files aroused suspicion among the auditors.  丢失的文件引起了审计人员的怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 encourage</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，激励；促进</td><td>encourage students to read<br>鼓励学生阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers encourage students to ask difficult questions.  好老师鼓励学生提出有难度的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excite</td><td>/ɪkˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，引发；使兴奋</td><td>excite public interest<br>激起公众兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech excited strong resentment among the workers.  他的演讲激起了工人们的强烈不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fire</td><td>/ˈfaɪər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>解雇；开火；火</td><td>fire an idle worker<br>解雇懒散的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager fired two clerks after the audit found losses.  审计查出亏损后经理解雇了两名职员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 impassion</del></td><td>/ˌɪmˈpæʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使激动，激起热情</td><td>impassion the crowd<br>使人群激动起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His closing speech impassioned even the most doubtful listeners.  他的结束演讲连最怀疑的听众都被激起热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incite</td><td>/ɪnˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>incite a riot<br>煽动暴乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was charged with inciting a riot outside the county courthouse.  他被指控在县法院外煽动暴乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 instigate</td><td>/ˈɪnstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>instigate a rebellion<br>煽动叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several officers were accused of helping to instigate the rebellion in the province.  几名军官被指控参与煽动该省的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 move</td><td>/ˈmuːv/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>感动，激起情感</td><td>move the audience deeply<br>深深感动观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final scene moved the entire audience to tears.  最后一幕让全场观众感动落泪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stimulate</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激发；促进</td><td>stimulate economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm rain stimulated the seeds to sprout within a week.  温暖的雨水刺激种子一周内发芽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stir</td><td>/ˈstɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，煽动；搅拌</td><td>stir up anger<br>激起怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The article stirred up anger among local residents.  这篇文章激起了当地居民的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 allay</td><td>/əˈleɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，平息</td><td>allay public fears<br>平息公众恐慌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The statement was meant to allay concerns about safety.  这份声明意在减轻人们对安全的担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 alleviate</td><td>/əˈliːvieɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>缓和，减轻</td><td>alleviate the pain<br>减轻疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A warm bath helped alleviate the soreness in his back.  热水澡帮他缓解了背部的酸痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
