<tr><td>↳近 plummet</td><td>/ˈplʌmɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>突然下降</td><td>plummet to a record low<br>暴跌至历史低点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Share prices plummeted to a record low after the announcement.  公告发布后，股价暴跌至历史低点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sink</td><td>/ˈsɪŋk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>下沉，下降；水槽</td><td>sink below the horizon<br>沉到地平线下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old boat began to sink soon after the storm.  暴风雨过后不久那条旧船开始下沉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumble</td><td>/ˈtʌmbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，杂乱一堆</td><td>a tumble of clothes<br>一堆乱衣服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was a tumble of boxes, books and old toys.  屋里是一堆乱七八糟的箱子、书和旧玩具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 skyrocket</td><td>/ˈskaɪˌrɑːkət/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>飞涨，猛升</td><td>prices skyrocket overnight<br>价格一夜飞涨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rents skyrocketed after the new factory opened downtown.  新工厂在市中心开业后房租飞涨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soar</td><td>/ˈsɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>猛增，飞升</td><td>costs soar<br>成本猛增</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building costs soared during the second year of construction.  施工第二年建筑成本猛增。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>polarize</b></td><td>/ˈpoʊləraɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使分开对立，使两极分化</td><td>polarize public opinion<br>使公众意见两极分化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposed law polarized public opinion within a single week.  这项法律提案在一周之内就使公众意见两极分化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bifurcate</td><td>/ˈbaɪfərkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>分叉，分成两支</td><td>bifurcate into two branches<br>分成两条支流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path bifurcates into two branches just beyond the wooden bridge.  这条小路在木桥之后分成两条岔路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diverge</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（使）分叉，散开；分歧</td><td>diverge from the path<br>偏离原有路径</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two trails diverge just beyond the old wooden bridge.  两条小径在那座老木桥之后就分岔开来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 divide</td><td>/dɪˈvaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>分开，划分；分歧</td><td>divide into groups<br>分成小组</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher divided the class into four groups of five.  老师把班级分成四个五人小组。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 coalesce</td><td>/ˌkoʊəˈles/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>合并，融合</td><td>coalesce into a group<br>合并成一个团体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scattered protests coalesced into a national movement.  分散的抗议汇合成一场全国性运动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unite</td><td>/juˈnaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>联合，团结</td><td>unite against a threat<br>联合抵御威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crisis united the villagers as nothing else had.  这场危机以前所未有的方式团结了村民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>polemic</b></td><td>/pəˈlemɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>激烈的论战；抨击文章</td><td>a fierce polemic<br>一场激烈的论战</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His essay turned into a fierce polemic against the city council.  他的文章变成了对市议会的猛烈抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contention</td><td>/kənˈtenʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>争执，争论；论点</td><td>a point of contention<br>争议之点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water rights remain a point of contention between the villages.  水权仍是各村之间的争议之点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 controversy</td><td>/ˈkɑːntrəˌvɜːrsi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>争论，争议</td><td>spark a controversy<br>引发一场争议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting sparked a controversy that lasted for decades.  这幅画引发了持续数十年的争议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disagreement</td><td>/dɪsəˈɡriːmənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，不一致，争执</td><td>a sharp disagreement<br>激烈的分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long disagreement over funding stalled the whole project.  关于资金的长期分歧使整个项目停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disputation</td><td>/ˌdɪspjuˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>辩论，争论</td><td>endless disputation<br>无休止的争辩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scholars spent the evening in learned disputation about grammar.  学者们整晚就语法进行博学的争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agreement</td><td>/əˈɡriːmənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，同意；协议</td><td>come to an agreement<br>达成协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two firms signed an agreement to share research costs.  两家公司签署协议分担研究费用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>polished</b></td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的</td><td>polished manners<br>优雅得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polished manners impressed everyone at the formal dinner.  他优雅得体的举止让正式晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cultivated</td><td>/ˈkʌltɪveɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有修养的</td><td>a cultivated taste in art<br>高雅的艺术品位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her cultivated manners impressed everyone at the dinner.  她优雅的举止让晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cultured</td><td>/ˈkʌltʃərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有修养的</td><td>a cultured gentleman<br>有教养的绅士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a cultured man who read poetry in four languages.  他是个有教养的人，能读四种语言的诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 genteel</td><td>/dʒenˈtiːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，不粗俗的</td><td>genteel manners<br>有教养的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her genteel accent betrayed an expensive and very traditional upbringing.  她文雅的口音透露出昂贵而传统的成长背景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refined</td><td>/rəˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精炼的，纯净的；有教养的</td><td>refined sugar<br>精制糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refined sugar has been stripped of every impurity.  精制糖已去除了所有杂质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 urbane</td><td>/ərˈbeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彬彬有礼的，文雅的</td><td>an urbane host<br>文雅的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our urbane host guided the conversation with easy wit and grace.  我们文雅的主人以轻松的机智和风度引导着谈话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gauche</td><td>/ɡoʊʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，缺乏社交经验的</td><td>a gauche remark<br>笨拙失礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His gauche remark about her salary embarrassed the whole table.  他关于她薪水的笨拙失礼的话让整桌人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rustic</td><td>/ˈrʌstɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>乡下人，头脑简单的人；粗俗的</td><td>rustic manners<br>粗俗的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His rustic manners amused the guests at the elegant city dinner.  他粗俗的举止让城里雅致晚宴上的客人觉得好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 philistine</td><td>/ˈfɪlɪstiːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>市侩（注重物质而鄙视智慧或艺术的人）</td><td>a cultural philistine<br>文化上的市侩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed modern poetry as nonsense, revealing himself a complete philistine.  他把现代诗贬为胡言乱语，暴露出自己是个十足的市侩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>politic</b></td><td>/ˈpɑːlətɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合时宜的，明智的，高情商的</td><td>a politic silence<br>明智的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would not be politic to mention his failure at the dinner.  在晚宴上提他的失败并不明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advisable</td><td>/ədˈvaɪzəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，可取的</td><td>it seems advisable<br>这似乎是明智的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is advisable to book your tickets several weeks early.  提前几周订票是明智的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 desirable</td><td>/dɪˈzaɪrəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可取的，明智合宜的</td><td>a desirable compromise<br>可取的折中方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Delaying the vote seemed the most desirable course available.  推迟投票似乎是最可取的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tactical</td><td>/ˈtæktɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>策略上明智的；战术的</td><td>a tactical retreat<br>策略性的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Staying silent was a tactical choice, not weakness.  保持沉默是策略上的选择，而非软弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imprudent</td><td>/ɪmˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的</td><td>an imprudent investment<br>不明智的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing so much money at that rate was clearly imprudent.  以那样的利率借这么多钱显然是不明智的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inadvisable</td><td>/ˌɪnədˈvaɪzəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，不可取的</td><td>inadvisable at this stage<br>现阶段不可取</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors said surgery was inadvisable for a patient so weak.  医生说对如此虚弱的病人手术并不可取。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 injudicious</td><td>/ˌɪndʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，欠考虑的</td><td>an injudicious remark<br>欠考虑的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One injudicious comment cost him the whole negotiation.  一句欠考虑的话让他输掉了整场谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwise</td><td>/ʌnˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，愚蠢的</td><td>an unwise decision<br>不明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing more money now would be deeply unwise.  现在再借钱是很不明智的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pompous</b></td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affected</td><td>/əˈfektɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>装出来的，做作的</td><td>an affected accent<br>装出来的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke with an affected accent that fooled nobody present.  她用装出来的口音说话，没骗过在场任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bombastic</td><td>/bɑːmˈbæstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸大的，浮夸的</td><td>bombastic rhetoric<br>浮夸的辞令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bombastic speech impressed nobody in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲没有打动任何听众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flowery</td><td>/ˈflaʊəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辞藻华丽的；多花的</td><td>flowery language<br>辞藻华丽的语言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letter was written in absurdly flowery language.  这封信用了荒唐华丽的辞藻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grandiloquent</td><td>/ɡrænˈdɪləkwənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张的，说大话的</td><td>grandiloquent promises<br>夸张的许诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters grew tired of his grandiloquent promises about reform.  选民厌倦了他关于改革的夸张许诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 magniloquent</del></td><td>/mæɡˈnɪləkwənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张的，辞藻浮夸的</td><td>a magniloquent style<br>浮夸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The preface is written in a magniloquent, old-fashioned style.  这篇序言以浮夸而老派的风格写成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rhetorical</td><td>/rɪˈtɔːrɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>言辞华丽（却往往无内涵）的</td><td>empty rhetorical flourishes<br>空洞的华丽辞藻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech was full of rhetorical flourishes but offered no concrete plan.  他的演讲满是华丽辞藻，却没有提出任何具体计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrogant</td><td>/ˈærəɡənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自大的</td><td>an arrogant manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrogant remarks cost him the respect of his colleagues.  他傲慢的言论使他失去了同事的尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 egocentric</td><td>/ˌiːɡoʊˈsentrɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自我中心的，自私的</td><td>an egocentric young star<br>自我中心的年轻明星</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His egocentric behavior slowly drove his teammates away.  他自我中心的行为慢慢把队友都赶走了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haughty</td><td>/ˈhɔːti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，目中无人的</td><td>a haughty manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the waiter a haughty look and said nothing.  她傲慢地看了服务员一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pontifical</td><td>/pɑnˈtɪfəkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自大浮夸的；教皇的</td><td>a pontifical tone<br>自大浮夸的口气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered every question in a pontifical tone that irritated us.  他用自大的口气回答每个问题，令我们厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presumptuous</td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冒失的，放肆的</td><td>presumptuous of you<br>你太放肆了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seems presumptuous to criticize a book you have not read.  批评一本没读过的书，似乎太放肆了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supercilious</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高傲的，傲慢的</td><td>a supercilious smile<br>傲慢的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A supercilious smile crossed his face when he heard my question.  听到我的问题，他脸上掠过傲慢的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 homely</td><td>/ˈhoʊmli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>其貌不扬的，朴素简单的</td><td>a homely face<br>其貌不扬的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a homely but kind face that put strangers at ease.  她有一张其貌不扬却和善的脸，让陌生人感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpretentious</td><td>/ˌʌnprɪˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不炫耀的，朴实低调的</td><td>an unpretentious restaurant<br>朴实的小餐馆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They live in a small, unpretentious house near the river.  他们住在河边一间朴实的小房子里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mind</td><td>/ˈmaɪnd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>留意，注意；介意</td><td>mind the step<br>留意台阶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please mind the gap between the train and the platform.  请注意列车与站台之间的空隙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
